# Arxiv Papers in cs.CV on 2026-11-19
### VoxForge: A Unified Foundation Model for Dense 3D Features Across MRI, CT and PET
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25309v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25309v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 00:09:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 00:09:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Rushil Anirudh, Jayaraman J. Thiagarajan
- **Comment**: 34 pages (8-page main paper, references, and 23-page supplementary material), 7 figures. Rushil Anirudh and Jayaraman J. Thiagarajan contributed equally
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A unified 3D medical foundation model promises reusable, frozen features that serve a wide variety of tasks such as segmentation, classification and registration on MRI, CT and PET as well as specialist models do. Generalist models still need separate backbones for localized and global tasks, specialist models are confined to a single modality, with the concern that spanning modalities under-emphasizes fine anatomy, and both typically rely on end-to-end fine-tuning to be competitive. We present VoxForge, a single encoder that integrates recent advances in 3D architecture, self-distillation and cross-modal curricula to learn transferable features from permissively licensed MRI and CT volumes. We evaluate it strictly frozen, the test a reusable foundation model must pass, against state-of-the-art generalist and specialist models on segmentation, classification and registration across MRI and CT, and on PET, which it never sees. VoxForge has the best or tied-best average rank in every task group, serves localized and global tasks across modalities at the level of state-of-the-art specialists, and is extremely label-efficient, matching top-performing models on TotalSegmentator CT with a tenth of the labels.



### MoGloss: Joint Motion and Pseudo-Gloss Pretraining for American Sign Language Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25333v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25333v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 01:16:07+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 01:16:07+00:00
- **Authors**: Rui Hong, Jana Košecká
- **Comment**: 15 pages, 4 figures. Project page: https://hongrui16.github.io/MoGloss
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Sign language translation and recognition have been shown to benefit from various pretraining approaches. We propose a novel two-stage pretraining approach for the motion encoder, which first learns to reconstruct masked motion, followed by joint masked prediction of pseudo-gloss units and motion. Pseudo-glosses serve as an intermediate structured representation of natural language text and are obtained from the captions by a large language model. We curate a corpus of about 1,100 hours of American Sign Language from five public datasets, estimate 2D pose for all clips and define an evaluation protocol. We then reproduce state-of-the-art translation methods and evaluate them using the same train and test splits. We propose a translation model built on our pretraining strategy, and this model outperforms the previous state-of-the-art methods Uni-Sign and SHuBERT, as well as the YouTube-ASL baseline, on the How2Sign, ASL STEM Wiki and OpenASL datasets. Furthermore, the gain from the joint pretraining using motion and pseudo-gloss grows as translation data shrinks. A classifier built on the same encoder also achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on ASL Citizen isolated sign recognition and outperforms our reproduction of Uni-Sign on WLASL2000.



### Breaking Noise Shortcuts in Self-Supervised Learning Via Noise-Aligned View Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25335v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **eess.IV**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25335v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 01:19:12+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 01:19:12+00:00
- **Authors**: Oankar R. Patil, May D. Wang
- **Comment**: 40th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2026)
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Self-supervised learning (SSL) is increasingly applied to scientific imaging domains, such as microscopy, where labels are scarce but noisy data is abundant. These domains exhibit significant signal-dependent noise, which can create a representational shortcut: since both augmented views derive from the same noisy image, the encoder can boost view agreement by also encoding noise patterns rather than semantic content. To enable noise-robust representation learning without knowledge of underlying noise models, we introduce Self-Aligned Noise Augmentation (SEANA), a drop-in module that generates noise-aware SSL views. SEANA learns an invertible variance-stabilizing transform (VST) from noisy data, then estimates the clean signal and resamples independent noise in learned VST space, requiring no clean targets, no dataset-specific denoiser training or use at inference, and no changes to the SSL objective or encoder. On CIFAR-10 and ImageNet-100 under synthetic Gaussian, Poisson-Gaussian, and multiplicative noise, SEANA improves clean-test linear-probe accuracy across contrastive/non-contrastive SSL methods, indicating higher-quality representations. On real fluorescence imaging, SEANA improves Jurkat cell-cycle stage classification by up to 28.6pp over standard SSL and 27.5pp over denoiser-preprocessed baselines. In all settings, SEANA outperforms the denoiser-preprocessed SSL pipelines, demonstrating that learned VST-space noise-aligned resampling yields more robust representations than denoising alone. Code is available at: https://github.com/opatil31/SEANA



### VideoRNG: Relightable Neural Gaussians from Generative Video Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25338v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25338v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 01:25:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 01:25:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Bowen Xue, Saeed Hadadan, Fabrice Rousselle, Ravi Ramamoorthi, Zahra Montazeri, Miloš Hašan
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Generating realistic 3D assets remains constrained by classical representations: surface meshes and compact physically based materials struggle to express objects whose appearance arises from fine geometry or complex light transport, while existing Gaussian-splatting generators typically bake fixed illumination into appearance. We introduce VideoRNG, a two-stage method that generates complete, relightable 3D assets from a text prompt or a single image without prescribing a mesh or analytic material model. A video generator fine-tuned from a pretrained video diffusion backbone first produces a structured RGBA turntable sequence under a known pose, camera, and illumination. A feed-forward large reconstruction model initialized from a pretrained video diffusion backbone then maps the video-VAE latent frames of selected turntable frames directly to millions of anisotropic Gaussians with learned neural appearance features. A shared directional decoder and stochastic Gaussian ray tracer turn this representation, which we call ray-traced Relightable Neural Gaussians (rtRNG), into radiance under novel viewpoints and illumination. To preserve high-frequency appearance while learning geometry, we introduce depth-anchored appearance training, and we make million-Gaussian reconstruction practical through foreground token selection and cost-aware distributed sampling. VideoRNG generates complex assets such as fur, feathers, and spider webs that can be interactively rendered, relit, and integrated into scenes.



### SceneAnything: Agentic Reconstruction of Large-Scale Mesh Scenes from a Single Video
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25340v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25340v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 01:26:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 01:26:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Bowen Xue, Saeed Hadadan, Fabrice Rousselle, Zahra Montazeri, Ravi Ramamoorthi, Milos Hasan
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reconstructing an editable 3D scene from video requires matching visible structure while plausibly completing unseen surfaces. Agents can write scene-construction programs, but direct reconstruction attempts often produce errors in layout, scale, and lighting. We introduce SceneAnything, an automated system for reconstructing mesh scenes from real and generated videos, enabling text-to-scene construction when paired with video generation. A software harness coordinates agents, pretrained vision models, and graphics tools through an explicit reconstruction protocol. Shared camera, depth, and segmentation estimates ground component construction: a scene-owner agent plans the environment and briefs authors, who write parameterized Blender programs. A visibility- and fit-based level-of-detail rule selects the construction level, while unseen regions are completed and marked as inferred. Assembled scenes use fitted lighting and supported motion, and revisions undergo programmatic checks and independent visual comparisons. Accepted programs and lessons are reused across scenes. We evaluate on 100 scenes; subset comparisons show improved original-view fidelity over direct-agent reconstruction and higher novel-view content similarity than the compared video-to-3D methods. We will release the harness, agent instructions, reusable libraries, and scenes.



### Search or Scoring? Diagnosing Why Visual World-Model Planners Fail
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25347v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25347v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 01:38:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 01:38:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Haoxuan Jia, Yang Liu, Shang Luo, Chenhao Wu, Zixiang Ni, Yingguang Yang, Feng Zhang, Chongyang Zhang, Hao Zheng, Kefu Xu, Congjing Ran, Jifeng Zhu, Bin Chong
- **Comment**: 43 pages, 10 figures, 30 tables; supplementary material included
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual world models such as DINO-WM and JEPA-WM plan with an optimizer that searches for actions whose predicted outcome has the lowest cost, its distance to a goal image. A plan can thus fail in two ways: the optimizer never tries a good action that the cost would prefer (a search failure), or the cost, computed on predictions, ranks a bad action first (a scoring failure). The two need different remedies, more search or a better model, but a planner does not record which one occurred. We tell them apart by executing the optimizer's candidates, scoring a known solution with the planner's own cost, and comparing costs on predicted and real outcomes. An error model explains when each arises and so how to use retrieved demonstration windows (DemoProp): start the search from them (a warm start) when prediction errors are small, and average them without the model when large errors make the optimizer pick overrated actions. Across four simulated environments and three world models, on short tasks whose goals the planner can represent, PushT and Wall fail mostly by search, while Metaworld and RoboCasa fail by scoring because the cost, sound on real outcomes (AUROC 0.94 on Metaworld), is at chance on predictions (0.54). The diagnosis tells which remedy pays off: a four times larger search lowers the real-outcome cost only where failures come from search (clearly for DINO-WM), and where they come from scoring, averaging the windows without the model solves 127 of 168 new Metaworld tasks, against at most 87 when the model chooses among them. Within PushT, degrading only the model flips which use is better, and our rule, frozen in advance, follows the flip and solves 678 of 960 new episodes, against 530 for the best fixed use.



### Fold the Loop: Decoding-Time KV Memory for Looped Vision-Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25369v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25369v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:06:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-20 03:06:27+00:00
- **Authors**: Kun Zhou, Wenmian Yang, Zhensheng Wang, Yanzeng Li, Weijia Jia
- **Comment**: v2: Added author affiliations and postal codes
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Looped vision-language models (VLMs) deepen visual reasoning by repeatedly applying weight-tied modules. However, their decoding memory grows with recurrent depth and limits the image size and batch size that fit in a fixed memory budget. Each recurrent step produces its own key-value (KV) states, so the cache holds several copies of the same image. We find that, after an exact prefill, earlier steps still need access to visual information but can reuse the final visual KV within the same module. We propose FoldCache, which compresses visual KV along the recurrent axis while preserving the full prefill computation. FoldCache partitions recurrent steps within each module into groups and retains the final visual KV of each group for its members to share during decoding. Sharing stays within module boundaries, while question and answer KV remain separate for each step. The grouping granularity controls the degree of compression, and folding can be combined with token eviction and quantization to further reduce memory use. On LoopVL, FoldCache approximately halves prefix KV memory while matching full-cache accuracy on five open-ended benchmarks. These memory savings support batch sizes up to 1.7 times those of a full cache under the same GPU memory budget, and up to 2.6 times with the more aggressive FoldCache-Lite.



### A Unified Framework for Image Manipulation Localization Under Multi-Type Weak Supervision
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25371v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25371v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:08:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 02:08:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Songlin Li, Hui Ma, Zhiqing Guo, Dan Ma, Gaobo Yang, Xin Liao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Existing weakly supervised image manipulation localization (IML) methods typically rely on annotation-specific training strategies, hindering the joint use of diverse annotations within a single model. This limits the scale of usable training data and constrains further progress. We propose UniIML, a teacher-student framework that maps diverse weak annotations into a unified representation to guide the Segment Anything Model (SAM) in generating multiple candidate masks. However, SAM is designed for general-purpose segmentation and has limited sensitivity to manipulation traces; its predicted quality scores alone may therefore fail to reliably identify the candidate that best delineates the manipulated region. We introduce Ordinal Safe Consensus (OSC), which first filters candidates using trusted weak-annotation constraints encoded in the unified representation. It then ranks the remaining candidates separately by consistency with weak annotations, SAM-predicted quality scores, and cross-view stability, aggregating these rankings for an initial selection. To assess how changes in the evaluation criteria affect candidate selection, OSC repeats ranking and selection while omitting one criterion at a time. The most frequently selected candidate is adopted as the final mask, and its relative selection frequency quantifies selection stability. We construct soft pseudo-labels from the selected candidate's predicted probabilities and estimate pixel-level supervision weights based on selection stability, pixel-level agreement among candidates, and predictive certainty. The student receives direct supervision in regions defined by weak annotations and learns from soft pseudo-labels elsewhere through reliability-weighted distillation, enabling unified training across weak annotation types. Experiments show that UniIML achieves superior average performance compared with state-of-the-art IML methods.



### Text Lives in the Guidance: Preventing Silent Text Rewriting in Cached Diffusion Transformers
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25382v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25382v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:23:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-20 03:06:31+00:00
- **Authors**: Kun Zhou, Wenmian Yang, Zhensheng Wang, Weijia Jia
- **Comment**: v2: Added author affiliations and postal codes
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Diffusion transformers (DiTs) now render long, legible text in images, and feature caching is a standard way to accelerate them. Caches are judged by image-level metrics, which do not check whether the rendered text is still correct. We find that caching silently rewrites this text. On Z-Image, TaylorSeer at 2.9x fewer FLOPs lowers word accuracy from 62% to 46%, while ImageReward and CLIP Score barely change. Sampling with fewer steps at the same compute keeps almost all of this text, so the loss comes from how the cache predicts the skipped steps. Decomposing the cache error along classifier-free guidance (CFG), we trace this loss mainly to the guidance term, the difference between the conditional and unconditional predictions, which caches extrapolate worst on text. Correcting the guidance inside the text alone recovers most of the lost text. We therefore propose GuideCache, which recomputes both CFG branches only on the tokens with the largest predicted guidance error, using the guidance terms that the cache already stores. At 2.4x fewer FLOPs, GuideCache restores full-compute prompt fidelity on Z-Image and Qwen-Image, and on Z-Image it renders text as well as or better than ToCa, TeaCache, and FasterCache while staying much closer to the full model's output. GuideCache works with caches of any order, and on top of fewer sampling steps it keeps more text than step reduction at a higher speedup. These results suggest that acceleration methods for text-rendering models should be evaluated on the text they render.



### Zero to Four Views: Unified Panorama Generation and Preference RL for Unobserved Regions
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25389v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25389v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:34:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 02:34:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Rui Hong, Xugang Ye, Jana Košecká, Kai Fan
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Panoramas can serve as shared scene backgrounds for multi-shot video generation, but most existing panorama generators take text alone or a single input image. We present a single model that generates a full equirectangular panorama from a text caption and K in {0,...,4} perspective conditioning views taken by a four-camera rig at one viewpoint. We fine-tune a pretrained text-to-image rectified-flow transformer in two stages. In the first stage, each conditioning view is appended to the token sequence together with the ID of its camera slot, and the number of views varies during training, so one set of weights serves every input setting. In the second stage, we improve the region not covered by any conditioning view. Supervised training computes its loss on noisy latents of the ground truth and never scores a panorama the model has generated and decoded, so we propose Uncovered-Region GRPO (UR-GRPO), a reinforcement-learning recipe that rewards the candidates a vision-language model prefers in pairwise comparisons on crops around this region. Because the slot ID alone does not tell the model which way the rig points, we also offer an optional variant that writes each view's camera direction into its position encoding, which lets the user decide where each view appears in the output. With one conditioning view, our model outperforms the previous state of the art, 360Anything, trained on the same data and evaluated with the same code, on Laval Indoor and gives mixed results on an indoor subset of SUN360; the second stage improves the results for every number of conditioning views.



### HOPD: Historical On-Policy Distillation for SAR MLLM Continual Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25391v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25391v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:34:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 02:34:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuxuan Li, Derrick Goh Xin Deik, Zheng-Peng Duan, Qianru Sun, Weijie Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Continual SAR interpretation requires multimodal large language models to learn incoming sources while retaining earlier task abilities. This is difficult when acquisition and geographic shifts alter visual evidence, regionally concentrated categories bias answers, and historical images are scarce. We address this problem with Historical On-Policy Distillation (HOPD), which reuses source-acquisition LoRA states as persistent teachers for one evolving student. Archiving each state before consolidation preserves a stable source reference. The teachers supervise student-generated prefixes, transferring historical behavior to the student's changing generation contexts. To enrich sparse replay, original-view teachers guide augmented historical student views while preserving spatial coordinates. This knowledge is consolidated into a single deployed adapter that requires no source identity. On SARVQA-CL, our source-incremental evaluation protocol, HOPD achieves the highest final mean score of 54.51 among the compared methods, exceeding the strongest baseline by 2.96 points. It also improves the final source-macro score in every evaluated task over shared-memory Replay-SFT, with gains across source orders. These results support source-acquisition history as supervision for retaining earlier task performance during continual SAR adaptation. Code and data: https://github.com/zcablii/HOPD.



### Decision Boundaries Matter: Graph-Regularized Residual Adaptation for Cross-Domain Fake Image Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25406v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25406v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:48:25+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-20 11:30:50+00:00
- **Authors**: Shichuang Xie, Qishuai Ding, Tong Qiao, Liquan Shen, Guorui Feng
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Despite learning transferable features, fake image detectors may struggle on unseen domains due to mismatched decision boundaries. Our analysis shows that features extracted by the frozen forensic encoder remain discriminative, but the source-trained decision boundary does not fully exploit this information. However, correcting this boundary without source data or target labels remains challenging, as unreliable source predictions may lead to incorrect adaptation. To address this issue, we propose Graph-Regularized Residual Adaptation (GraRA), which refines the source decision boundary using only unlabeled target data. To determine which classifier directions to update, GraRA constructs a compact residual subspace from uncertainty-weighted target features and keeps it orthogonal to the source classification direction. To guide these updates, it uses source-score anchors to generate soft labels through target-graph diffusion, while neighborhood consistency regularizes residual learning within the constrained subspace. The learned correction is merged into the source classifier, requiring only 32 optimized coefficients and no additional inference branches. On diverse unseen OpenMMSec domains, GraRA achieves 84.36% accuracy and 91.34% AUC, improving over the source detector by 4.33 and 3.50 percentage points, respectively. Further analysis shows that reliable source predictions and locally consistent neighborhoods help guide residual learning, allowing GraRA to correct ranking errors that a simple threshold shift cannot address.



### FG-GraphBench: Benchmarking and Enhancing Fine-Grained Visual Understanding with Multimodal Scene Graphs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25408v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25408v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:49:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 02:49:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Sicheng Liu, Xunkai Li, Ziyu Han, Xingnan Zhang, Ru Zhang, Rong-Hua Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Fine-grained visual understanding requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to recognize small visual entities and understand their relationships. Scene graphs offer a natural way to represent this information, organizing visual entities as nodes and their relationships as edges. However, we identify two challenges: existing scene graph benchmarks provide insufficient coverage of fine-grained entities and the relationships among them, and existing methods for fine-grained visual understanding have yet to effectively leverage such graph-structured information in MLLMs. To address these challenges, we introduce Fine-Grained Multimodal Scene Graph Benchmark (FG-GraphBench) and FiGRA. FG-GraphBench is a benchmark comprising three complementary tasks: fine-grained visual question answering (FG-QA), image captioning (FG-Caption), and image description generation (FG-Description). We develop a two-stage construction pipeline that discovers and grounds fine-grained entities and establishes their relations to construct the fine-grained multimodal scene graphs (MSGs) in FG-GraphBench. Building on these MSGs, we propose FiGRA, a Fine-grained Graph Representation Augmentation method for MLLMs. FiGRA adopts a two-stage training strategy. It encodes multimodal entity and relation features as continuous graph tokens, and integrates them into the MLLM. Experiments show that FiGRA significantly outperforms strong baselines across three tasks of FG-GraphBench. Further analyses confirm the effectiveness of FG-GraphBench and highlight the importance of preserving fine-grained entity and relation information for visual understanding.



### Watertight Mesh Optimization of Textured Objects
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25414v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25414v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:53:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 02:53:08+00:00
- **Authors**: Nathaniel Burgdorfer, Philippos Mordohai
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: When reconstructing high-quality 3D assets from images, existing approaches often excel at one objective while compromising another, and frequently overlook the intrinsic quality of the reconstructed surfaces. We present MODL, a framework that jointly optimizes geometry and view-dependent appearance directly on a watertight triangle mesh. Our approach combines Laplacian-preconditioned optimization for smooth vertex updates with curvature-adaptive remeshing to preserve fine details and well-shaped triangles. The triangle mesh is optimized subject to multi-view normalized cross-correlation constraints, but without segmentation, geometric supervision or alpha-blending. Evaluations on object reconstruction datasets demonstrate that MODL achieves competitive novel-view synthesis while producing geometrically accurate models with superior intrinsic mesh quality. Our results highlight the potential of direct mesh optimization to unify photorealistic rendering, geometric reconstruction, and practical 3D asset creation.



### MIR-Depth: Multi-Range Refinement for Single-View RGB-D Depth Completion of Transparent Objects
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25417v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25417v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:58:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-20 12:26:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Xinzhe Fu, Qi Shao, Yuanfei Xie, Taoyu Wu, Zhihao Cao, Long Hu, Hong Liang, Xianzhi Li, Anh Nguyen, Baoru Huang, Rongsheng Li
- **Comment**: 9 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recovering accurate visible-surface depth from a single RGB-D image and an object mask is essential for robotic grasping and manipulation of transparent objects. Specular reflections cause missing depth, while refraction corrupts nonzero returns with background depth, making raw measurements unreliable as surface anchors. Under a fixed sampling budget, refining an initial single-view prediction within a single search interval either misses large refraction offsets or sacrifices fine local resolution. To resolve this single-view trade-off, MIR-Depth constructs Coordinate-Aware Multi-Range Hypotheses: a 3D-aggregated global inverse-depth volume and depth-axis-filtered pixel-wise local volumes around a prior-guided initial depth. Selective Recurrent Refinement then introduces out-of-bounds lookup masking and validity-masked softmax fusion to iteratively update inverse depth from these volumes using a multi-scale ConvGRU. Finally, Spatially Gated Correction applies a learned per-pixel spatial gate to the full-resolution residual, preserving accurate initial geometry without imposing raw sensor readings as hard constraints. Experiments on TransCG, TODD, TRansPose, ClearPose, and HouseCat6D show that MIR-Depth achieves lower error than ReMake, DFNet, BP-Net, and PSD. Real-robot evaluations confirm accurate surface geometry for 6-DoF grasp pose prediction. Code and pretrained models will be publicly available.



### Bilateral Evidence Selection and Reasoning for Mammography Vision--Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25418v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25418v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:58:29+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 02:58:29+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhengbo Zhou, Dooman Arefan, Shandong Wu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Medical vision--language models (VLMs) often need to identify small, subtle lesions within high-resolution images containing extensive normal anatomy and redundant visual information. Visual-token compression improves efficiency, but preserving salient regions alone can discard the context needed for diagnostic interpretation. This context can extend across images: in bilateral mammography, the opposite breast provides a patient-specific anatomical reference. Yet retaining such evidence does not ensure that the model uses it effectively. We propose Bilateral Evidence Selection and Reasoning (BESR), which connects evidence preservation with comparative assessment through a bilateral-guided token selector and a bilateral reward. Under a shared token budget, the selector jointly prioritizes lesion appearance and its local and bilateral context, while the reward encourages assessments that benefit from the preserved comparison. Experiments on a bilateral dataset constructed from CBIS-DDSM and on VinDr-Mammo demonstrate improved evidence retention and diagnostic performance under constrained visual-token budgets. Inference benchmarking further shows reduced prefill latency.



### DiLE: Discrimination-Aware Latent-Space Learning for Universal Multimodal Embeddings
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25419v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25419v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 02:59:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 02:59:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Ben Kang, Junda Wang, Yuxuan Yuan, Hang Li, Yuhan Liu, Xin Chen, Wanting Geng, Tong Lin, Pei Fu, Dong Wang, Jian Luan, Huchuan Lu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal large language models have greatly improved input understanding in universal multimodal embedding models. However, this understanding does not ensure that their embeddings preserve the evidence needed to distinguish relevant items from confusable alternatives. To preserve essential discriminative evidence in embeddings, this paper proposes DiLE, a discrimination-aware latent-space learning framework that combines task guidance with on-policy retrieval feedback. Specifically, a few learnable queries are used to encode task-relevant information in a compact latent space. Reasoning instructions and auxiliary answer-generation supervision shape these representations to support both task understanding and retrieval discrimination. To address task-specific discriminative needs, we design shared-private expert adaptation that integrates cross-task knowledge sharing with task-specific representation learning. Building on this, the on-policy hard-negative distillation is introduced: the student retrieves confusable candidates, and a reverse KL divergence objective aligns its embedding-based similarity distribution with the teacher's fine-grained relevance distribution over these candidates. Candidates are periodically refreshed using updated student snapshots, tying teacher supervision to the student's evolving retrieval behavior and confusions. Across four model configurations that span two backbone generations, DiLE achieves state-of-the-art performance on MMEB-v2, UVRB, and MRMR, demonstrating its effectiveness across modalities and tasks.



### Raptor: Rejection-aware Adaptive Tree based on Speculative Decoding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25436v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25436v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:17:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:17:52+00:00
- **Authors**: Qishu Hu, Haozhao Wang, Zicong Hong, Yufeng Zhan, Yuanqing Xia
- **Comment**: 18pages, 7figures, 3tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Tree-structured speculative decoding accelerates vision-language models (VLMs) by verifying multiple candidate continuations in parallel. However, long visual contexts increase the cost of speculative computation, making candidate coverage and redundant expansion important considerations beyond tree topology adaptation. We propose Raptor, a training-free framework that uses rejection feedback to jointly regulate candidate coverage and drafting effort. Our key insight is that acceptance length alone cannot distinguish insufficient candidate coverage from excessive deep expansion, and these conditions require different allocation decisions. Raptor therefore extracts complementary rejection-aware signals to characterize speculative progress, frontier coverage, and redundant expansion. These signals jointly guide bounded, momentum-smoothed updates of tree depth, branching width, and drafting budget, together with execution control that limits unnecessary drafting and progressively restores expansion when appropriate. Extensive experiments with two VLMs across seven multimodal benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of Raptor, achieving up to 2.39x average speedup over autoregressive decoding.



### Wearable Logo, Silent Hijack: Stealthy Cross-Modal Semantic Attacks on Visible--Infrared Vision-Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25437v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25437v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:18:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:18:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Qike Zhang, Jiaju Han, Xuelian Shi, Xiang Chen, Dehe Zhao, Banglong Nong, Wenhao Zheng, Lei Wang, Jiahuan Long, Jiujiang Guo, Chengyin Hu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Logos appear naturally on clothing and daily necessities, where their recognizable shapes and semantic associations make them familiar visual cues. This familiarity helps them blend into real-world environments and remain visually inconspicuous. We ask whether an existing logo can serve as a carrier for coordinated targeted semantic attacks on visible-infrared (VIS-IR) vision-language models (VLMs) without changing its original outline. This setting requires expressive adversarial variation within a fixed shape while accounting for different appearance characteristics and model responses across modalities. We propose Logo-guided Semantic Attack (LoSA), a score-based black-box framework that optimizes a logo's interior appearance and placement while preserving its silhouette. LoSA uses a contour field and low-frequency graph spectral modes to structure the interior color layout, a frozen appearance adapter to couple VIS and IR appearances, and particle swarm optimization to jointly optimize shared design parameters. Experiments across four datasets demonstrate that LoSA consistently outperforms the evaluated baselines in simultaneous Top-1 targeted success. Cross-architecture experiments, visual naturalness ratings, and captioning/VQA transfer analyses further characterize its effectiveness and limitations. These findings reveal that familiar visual symbols from real-world environments can serve as shape-preserving carriers for coordinated targeted semantic attacks across imaging modalities.



### VisualTalk-Bench: Evaluating Question-Driven Avatar-Led Video Explanations
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25444v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25444v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:24:26+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:24:26+00:00
- **Authors**: Xin Zhang, Tujie Xu, Zhiyi Chen, Xiaodong Zhang, Sicheng Yang, Minglei Li
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 3 figures, 4 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Advances in video generation and avatar synthesis enable new ways to answer user questions through visual explanations. This task requires a system to formulate a correct answer, translate relevant information into visual material, and deliver the explanation through an avatar's speech and actions. Existing benchmarks rarely assess whether avatars point to the visual content being discussed. We introduce VisualTalk-Bench for question-driven avatar-led video explanations in finance and Chinese and English education. It includes 800 curated reference videos and 100 independent evaluation requests, assessing Content Factuality, Speech-Visual Consistency, Visual Quality, Motion Smoothness, and Avatar-Content Pointing. Yet end-to-end video generators often struggle to align gestures with specific visual targets. To explore this challenge, we develop Avatar-O Agent, a modular reference implementation with explicit interaction planning. Experiments across six agents show that factual precision and visual quality do not guarantee accurate pointing. A 26-participant study comparing four agents finds that Avatar-O Agent receives the highest average ratings for visual guidance, perceived pointing accuracy, and explanation effectiveness. We will release the data and code.



### Stereo Visual Odometry with In-the-Loop Geometric Feed-Forward Models Towards Dense Metric Mapping
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25445v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25445v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:24:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:24:38+00:00
- **Authors**: Fahira Afzal Maken, Sagun Man Singh Shrestha, Edward Vanderfeen, Lucas Carvalho de Lima, Rongxin Li, Changming Sun, Milad Ramezani
- **Comment**: 8 PAGES, 6 FIGURES
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We present a stereo visual odometry (SVO) framework that integrates geometric feed-forward models (GFMs) for dense depth prediction with factor-graph-based visual odometry to achieve accurate localisation and dense metric mapping. Our key idea is to exploit sparse but reliable metric geometry estimated from stereo and temporal multi-view observations to calibrate dense GFM predictions, while retaining the robustness and efficiency of model-based SVO. Rather than directly conditioning the GFM on potentially noisy stereo depth, optimised 3D landmarks are used to jointly estimate a scale-shift transformation over all keyframes within each local submap, yielding metrically consistent dense depth predictions. To balance accuracy and computational efficiency, we introduce a hierarchical factor-graph architecture. At the local level, sliding-window optimisation estimates high-frequency camera poses and multi-view-refined 3D landmarks. Dense predictions are generated only for selected keyframes and registered within each submap using SE(3) Generalised ICP. Consecutive submaps are further aligned in Sim(3) using their shared geometry, allowing residual scale inconsistencies to be corrected. These intra- and inter-submap constraints are incorporated into a global factor graph to produce a consistent trajectory and dense metric reconstruction. Extensive experiments on public and in-house datasets demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves competitive odometry accuracy against geometric and learning-based baselines while providing accurate dense metric reconstruction and improved robustness to failures in learned depth prediction.



### Beyond Resemblance: Image Fusion Reimagined
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25453v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25453v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:32:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:32:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Hao Zhang, Yumin Huang, Linfeng Tang, Junjun Jiang, Jiayi Ma
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Image fusion often lacks ground-truth fused images, forcing existing deep methods to indirectly define fusion objectives by requiring the fused image to simultaneously resemble multiple source images. However, the distinct imaging characteristics of different modalities can impose competing constraints on the fused result, potentially limiting the effective integration of their complementary information. To address this long-standing challenge facing the image fusion community, we propose ApexFuse, a novel generative image fusion paradigm that moves beyond source-resemblance constraints toward learning a general distribution of high-quality scene representations. Specifically, we develop a human-LMM-in-the-loop pipeline that leverages large multimodal generative models and iterative human feedback to translate abstract fusion objectives into reliable visual references, thereby defining the target distribution of ideal fusion outcomes. Through target-distribution-guided adaptation, we transfer the rich generative priors of pretrained FLUX to image fusion, enabling high-quality scene reconstruction conditioned on multimodal observations. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ApexFuse harmoniously integrates salient thermal targets, natural colors, and fine textures, achieving superior visual quality as well as semantic segmentation and object detection performance over previous source-resemblance methods. Moreover, ApexFuse exhibits remarkable cross-task generalization, extending from infrared-visible fusion to multi-exposure fusion, multi-focus fusion, and visible-depth fusion without additional fine-tuning.



### Lossless Latent Diffusion Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25454v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25454v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:33:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:33:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Tongda Xu, Yu Zhang, Jun Hu, Huijuan Huang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We study the problem of training image diffusion models while preserving pixel-exact reconstruction. Prior works typically address this problem by directly modeling images in pixel space, requiring modifications to the diffusion training objective or model architecture. In this work, we introduce a different paradigm: rather than modifying diffusion models, we construct a lossless transformation that maps pixels into a representation more amenable to diffusion modeling. We term our approach the Lossless Latent Diffusion Model (LLDM). We construct LLDM using residual latent transformation and uneven latent scaling, and explain its effectiveness through a proposed metric, semantic rank priority (SRP). Empirically, LLDM substantially outperforms prior pixel-space diffusion models in generation quality and even surpasses several widely used lossy VAEs. Furthermore, we show that SRP is strongly correlated with downstream generative performance.



### CANDOR: Contrastive Self-Denoising Rewards for Label-Free Text-to-Image Post-Training
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25457v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25457v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:37:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:37:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Junhyeok Lee, Byung-Hoon Kim, Kyu Sung Choi
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 3 figures, 5 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Post-training text-to-image generators with reinforcement learning usually relies on external reward models that need annotated preferences or auxiliary evaluators and are prone to reward hacking. We show that the generator's own denoising accuracy, used by a recent intrinsic reward, is a poor proxy for prompt fidelity. Within a same-prompt group, this absolute self-confidence is dominated by image complexity and ranks correct samples above incorrect ones near chance. We introduce CANDOR (Contrastive Alignment from Null-referenced Denoising lOg-Ratio), the log-ratio of unconditional to conditional denoising errors under shared noise probes, which measures how much the prompt helps the model denoise its own sample. It cancels prompt-independent difficulty that scales both errors and ranks the better of two same-prompt samples higher in 60% and 63% of pairs on compositional generation and text rendering. As the only Flow-GRPO reward for a flow-matching model, CANDOR improves text rendering, aesthetic and human-preference scores over the base model and a prior absolute self-confidence reward, with text-rendering and preference gains that stronger classifier-free guidance does not reproduce. On public Flow-GRPO adapters trained on external rewards, CANDOR further raises PickScore and ImageReward, so it can complement those rewards. Code is available at https://github.com/jhlee0619/candor.



### RemoteVLPG: How to Obtain a Specific VLM for Language-Guided Path Generation on Remote Sensing Images?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25458v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25458v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:39:24+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:39:24+00:00
- **Authors**: Junzhe Dang, Mengmeng Zhang, Yuxiang Zhang, Wei Li
- **Comment**: 18 pages, 10 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We study how to obtain a task-specific Vision-Language Model (VLM) for language-guided path generation between start and end points on remote sensing images. We explore this question from three aspects. First, we implement three widely used architectural approaches and compare their performance. Second, we design a scalable method for constructing pre-training data and build an effective pre-training dataset with a degree of diversity. Methods trained on this dataset achieve promising zero-shot performance. Third, we construct three benchmarks that support stratified evaluation and design three metrics for path generation. We investigate this problem through extensive experiments. The results show that the zero-shot performance of methods trained on the constructed pre-training dataset can exceed that of some advanced commercial VLMs. Code and Dataset can be available at https://github.com/jzdang/RemoteVLPG



### ActMem: From Latent Memory Reasoning to Grounded Robotic Action
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25461v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25461v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:46:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:46:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Linfeng Dong, Jianwei Niu, Jiuyi Zhao, Ruibing Feng, Xinghao Wu, Wu Yang, Shuai Tao, Wulong Liu, Mu Li
- **Comment**: 17 pages, 11 figures, 6 tables. Code and checkpoints: https://github.com/LigphiDonk/ActMem
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have shown promising results in real-world long-horizon manipulation, where memory plays a crucial role.   However, existing memory-augmented VLAs either retrieve past observations without reasoning about what they imply, or reason over the full   history in a shared context where it crowds out current perception. Cognitive science treats them as distinct systems: memory reconstructs past   experience to decide what to do, while perception guides how to act. We therefore propose ActMem, an in-context memory framework that   decouples memory reasoning from perceptual grounding within a single vision-language backbone. Built on a vision-language model with native   video understanding, ActMem infers the current subtask from a timestamped interaction history and keeps the hidden states formed during this   inference as a Latent Memory Chain-of-Thought (Memory CoT), which conditions a flow-matching action expert. Perception Attention further lets   the expert attend directly to the current view. To keep inference fast under long histories, Efficient Memory CoT Inference reuses computation   across consecutive decisions without changing the output. ActMem achieves 71.1% task success and 80.8% completion success on RoboMemArena,   surpassing the strongest baseline by 7.5 and 8.7 percentage points, and 73.8% average success on MIKASA-Robo, the best among all compared   methods. In the real world, it reaches 68.8% average success on exogenous, endogenous, and traceless memory tasks, 10.0 percentage points above   the strongest memory-augmented VLA. Code and checkpoints will be released at https://github.com/LigphiDonk/ActMem and   https://huggingface.co/donk111/ActMem.



### A Wolf in Sheep's Pixels: Visual Camouflage Attacks via HDR Gain Maps
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25469v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25469v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 03:55:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 03:55:38+00:00
- **Authors**: Qi Shen, Yujia Gong, Yue Zhao, Tong Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: An image can pass automated safety checks while showing harmful content to its human viewer. We introduce DRJanus, a visual camouflage attack that exploits the gap between standard dynamic range (SDR) processing and high dynamic range (HDR) display. Automated systems often read only an image's SDR base, whereas HDR viewers reconstruct its appearance using an auxiliary gain map. DRJanus inverts this reconstruction to embed two independently chosen color images in a single JPEG, without querying or optimizing against the target model. It preserves the chosen SDR base exactly and reconstructs HDR targets at an average PSNR of 43.03 dB across 400 images. On a benchmark of 100 sexual-content images, DRJanus evades detection by all six tested commercial moderation services and nine vision-language models. Across five agents, each evaluated on 120 task cases per threat setting, attack success ranges from 31.67% to 100% for moderation evasion and from 95% to 100% for task manipulation. We also explore cross-view consistency checks as a mitigation. These findings expose a gap in visual trust: securing what a machine reads does not secure what a user sees. A demo is available at https://qshen0629.github.io/DRJanus/



### TuringPAI: A Foundation for On-Device Physical AI
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25474v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25474v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 04:01:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 04:01:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Qiman Wu, Hanlin Chen, Yuhua Wei, Mingyuan Wang, Shaman Tang, Minghao Li, Rui Xin, Xingye Liu, Zhichao Yang, Yiqi Yu, Hongyan Pei, Xiaowen Tian, YingYing Liu, Shiwen Liu, Jiahui Hu, Yizhao Wang, Yuheng Zhang, Anhua Liu, Yue He, Shuang Su, Da Zhu, Hua Zhou, Pengfei Diao, Haojie Yang, Weichao Huang, Hang Zhang, Honggou Yang, Xianming Liu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Physical AI requires models that understand spatial relationships and dynamic environments and translate observations into actions under limited onboard compute. We present TuringPAI, a family of models for on-device Physical AI comprising TuringPAI-VL for shared vision-language understanding, TuringPAI-Drive for autonomous driving, and TuringPAI-Embodied for robotic manipulation. TuringPAI-VL is a 0.65B model built on HybridViT-MLQ, which combines convolutional local aggregation with Transformer-based global modeling. Multi-scale Latent Queries integrate spatial features and observation history within the same visual Transformer, providing a compact, structured interface to the language model. Training combines general vision-language data with geometric, cross-view, and temporal supervision. TuringPAI-VL supports general multimodal understanding and improves quantitative geometric perception over Qwen2-VL-2B, while providing a shared initialization for the two downstream VLA models. For autonomous driving, TuringPAI-Drive maintains all evaluated ADE/FDE metrics within 1.2\% of a full-token HybridViT baseline using the MLQ interface and achieves comparable aggregate scores in an in-house simulation evaluation. Using XPeng Native Runtime (XNRT) on a single NVIDIA Orin-X SoC, TuringPAI-Drive generates trajectories at 12\,Hz in on-vehicle operation. For robotic manipulation, TuringPAI-Embodied achieves a 96.8\% average success rate across the four LIBERO suites. These results demonstrate a shared vision-language foundation for driving and robotic manipulation under practical on-device constraints.



### SegAnyFruit: Learning Generalizable and Edge Deployable Fruit Instance Segmentation at Scale
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25475v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25475v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 04:03:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 04:03:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Yanan Wang, Yuzheng wang, Yibin Ying, Zhenghao Fei
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 6 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Deploying fruit instance segmentation in real agricultural settings requires generalizing across unseen species, orchards, and imaging conditions under strict onboard compute and annotation budgets. We introduce SegAnyFruit (SAF), a data construction and progressive training framework that distills foundation-model priors into lightweight, prompt-free segmenters. SAF combines generic mask pre-training, broad mid-training on automatically annotated web imagery from our curated WebFruits dataset, and human-verified agricultural refinement post-training on PaperFruits, together spanning 733K images and 9.66M whole-fruit masks across 165 fruit types. Across seven benchmarks, zero-shot SAF-RF-DETR-XL reaches 69.06 average mask AP50:95 (+4.76 over SAM 3) while remaining deployable on edge devices. SAF initialization also boosts few-shot to full-shot adaptation across five agricultural domains while mitigating catastrophic forgetting. Finally, we systematically analyze training stages, data scale, model capacity, and generative augmentation, and validate our pipeline's transferability to underwater, cell, and human segmentation. We will release our datasets, code, and SAF models.



### TAP: Tapping the Discriminative Potential of Frozen Representations for Class-Incremental Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25478v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25478v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 04:08:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 04:08:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Zihao Wang, Yu-Wei Zhan, Xiao-Qian Liu, Zhen-Duo Chen, Weitong Liu, Xin Luo, Xin-Shun Xu
- **Comment**: 10 pages, 3 figures. Code is included as an ancillary file
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pre-trained models provide transferable representations for class-incremental learning (CIL). However, sequentially adapting these representations introduces optimization costs and may disrupt old-class feature structure. This motivates us to explore whether the discriminative potential of these representations can be further exploited by continually accumulating class information while keeping the encoders frozen. To this end, we propose TAP, a new and novel framework for tapping the discriminative potential of frozen representations in class-incremental learning. It accumulates visual and semantic class information in the frozen CLIP embedding space with only lightweight optimization. TAP comprises three modules: class-conditional visual distribution modeling, class-adaptive semantic calibration, and visual-semantic posterior fusion. The first module retains class means and within-class scatters, combining class-specific and shared covariances to stabilize distribution estimation. The second module learns class-specific weights over text templates from current-task images, adapting text representations to downstream classes. The third module combines the visual and semantic posteriors through a weighted product of experts for prediction over all seen classes without retaining images from previous tasks. Across nine datasets under two incremental protocols, TAP achieves state-of-the-art performance, attaining the highest average incremental and final-stage accuracies among the compared methods. The code is provided as an ancillary file.



### PRIO: Pre-trained Representation for Interactive Objects in 4D Worlds
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25500v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25500v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 04:45:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 04:45:52+00:00
- **Authors**: Shixun Huang, Lifu Wei, Yue Chang, Chen Geng, Heng Zhang, Jinhyung Ahn, Caoliwen Wang, Siyuan Chen, Donglai Xiang, Jiajun Wu, Peter Yichen Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D worlds can now be captured, reconstructed, and generated at scale, yet the objects in them stay still: a pressed pillow does not compress, and a pulled door does not swing. Bringing these objects into interactive 4D world models requires a representation of how they can move, deform, and respond physically to user actions in real time. We present PRIO, a pre-trained representation for interactive objects. Given static geometry, PRIO predicts an object-specific configuration space in a single forward pass. This space provides generalized coordinates for Lagrangian mechanics, allowing physical laws and user actions to govern motion. Configurations are linear in these coordinates, allowing the quantities needed by the dynamics to be precomputed. Dynamics then run at interactive rates without further network inference. Pre-trained on diverse 4D trajectories, PRIO learns priors over object configurations that generalize to unseen shapes. It supports meshes, point clouds, and 3D Gaussians, bringing objects in reconstructed and generated scenes into interaction. We demonstrate its use in real-to-sim workflows and robot manipulation. In addition, compared with existing 4D generation methods, PRIO generates dynamics faster and follows user actions more faithfully.



### Carry the Fine Print: Budget-Aware Visual Token Compression for Document OCR
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25506v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25506v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 04:54:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-20 03:32:50+00:00
- **Authors**: Yixuan Huang, ShengFan Zhang, Jiawei Chen
- **Comment**: 11 pages; supplementary material is included as an ancillary file
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Visual token compression often treats salience or similarity as a proxy for transcription importance. Complete document OCR breaks this assumption: small symbols and repeated entries can be indispensable to the transcript despite being visually inconspicuous. We present Select and Compensate, a two-stage framework built on a simple idea: choosing where to retain a token need not determine all the evidence it carries. Selection combines adaptive encoding resolution with global importance and local contrast to choose which tokens to retain as anchors. Compensation enriches these anchors with nearby discarded content and its relative position, without adding tokens. The OCR backbone remains frozen, and native coordinates and structural tokens are preserved. We evaluate complete page transcription across five backbones on OmniDocBench and document reconstruction on MPDocBench. With half the original visual tokens, our input-adapted configuration reaches 95.42 OmniDocBench Overall on Ovis versus 96.09 for the uncompressed model, and paired evaluations show gains over simply reducing the input resolution on both benchmarks. With the original input grid, our method stays within 0.17 points of the uncompressed model at 75% retention and exceeds a learned global selector by 4.70 points at 25%. On a fixed 200-page Ovis workload at the same 50% visual-token budget, only 3 of its outputs hit the length limit, versus 30-34 for the evaluated RTPrune, DivPrune, and HiPrune adaptations, and it finishes the batch 35-40% sooner. Code will be made publicly available.



### Persona Lenses: What Makes a Vision-Language Model See Through Its Persona's Eyes?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25508v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.HC
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25508v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 04:58:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 04:58:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Yoonho Shin
- **Comment**: 29 pages, 9 figures, 14 tables, including supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language models have been used to simulate where a user looks on a web page. However, a persona in the prompt reaches the simulated gaze only partly. We measure this reach as the difference in one model's gaze between two MatrAIx personas that differ in a single attribute. We introduce persona lenses, which place the persona between the model and the page: a text lens (a two-step prompt in which the model first writes what the persona would look at), a pixel lens (marks on the region matching the persona's interests) and a weight lens (a dwell reward gated to keep the scanpath natural). Using Qwen3-VL (4B and 32B) and Qwen3.5-9B on generated and live pages, we compare the lenses with one another and with scale, the 32B model with no lens. It has been confirmed that the text lens separates the personas strongly but is not robust to profile changes that should not move gaze. The pixel lens separates the personas less but is unaffected by attributes unrelated to gaze. At 4B and 9B, the weight lens separates the personas on interests held out from training, with no extra step on a new page. The 32B model separates the personas with the base prompt alone. Therefore, the choice of a lens depends on cost and model size.



### Life-IQA+: Boosting Blind Image Quality Assessment via GCN-enhanced Layer Interaction and Flexible Hierarchical Expert Specialization
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25510v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **eess.IV**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25510v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 05:00:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:00:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Long Tang, Guoquan Zheng, Huiyu Duan, Jie Hao, Yucheng Zhu, Liang Yuan, Xiongkuo Min, Guangtao Zhai
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Blind image quality assessment (BIQA) is essential for evaluating and optimizing visual experience. Existing BIQA methods typically aggregate features from multiple backbone stages without accounting for their unequal contributions to quality prediction. Furthermore, uniformly supervising all experts may constrain their flexibility and limit the diversity of the learned quality representations. To address these issues, we present Life-IQA+, a quality representation decoding framework that combines graph convolutional network (GCN) enhanced layer interaction and flexible hierarchical expert specialization. Specifically, the layer interaction module constructs quality queries from GCN-refined deepest-stage features and uses penultimate-stage features as keys and values, thereby enabling cross-layer representation interaction via the cross-attention mechanism. Building on these representations, we further introduce a flexible hierarchical mixture-of-experts (MoE) architecture comprising a densely activated shared expert layer and a sparsely activated routed expert layer. To enhance the representation diversity and ability of experts, we apply auxiliary classification supervision to a subset of shared experts using distortion-type labels for synthetic datasets and discretized quality-level labels for authentic datasets. Experiments on synthetic and authentic distortion benchmarks, together with controlled ablations of expert configurations and supervision strategies, show that Life-IQA+ achieves competitive prediction accuracy with a favorable balance between accuracy and computational cost. The code is available at: https://github.com/TANGLONG2/Life-IQA-PlusPlus.



### Mesh-Free Keypoint Learning for Category-Level 6D Object Pose Estimation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25513v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25513v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 05:03:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:03:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Junghoon Seo, Hakjin Lee, Jaehoon Sim
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Category-level 6D pose estimation relates partial RGB-D observations of unseen instances to a canonical frame shared across a category. Object-aware reconstruction helps adaptive keypoints retain the geometry of the visible surface. Common reconstruction targets select observed points by their distance to a registered instance surface. We present MORSEPose, which derives this support directly from standard pose and metric-size annotations. We map observed 3D points to normalized object coordinates and retain those inside a tolerancedilated pose-and-size envelope. This construction is consistent across camera frames and metric scales. When the annotated extent bounds the object surface, the envelope conservatively contains the support of a conventional surface-distance selector. We integrate this target with multi-level RGB-D features, adaptive keypoints, and learnable category context. Experiments on REAL275 and HouseCat6D show that the envelope closely matches surface-based supervision at both point and task levels, remains stable under boundary perturbations and viewpoint changes, and supports competitive category-level pose accuracy across benchmarks.



### Multi-View Relighting with Mergeable Radiance Fields
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25523v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25523v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 05:25:36+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:25:36+00:00
- **Authors**: Yinan Wang, Rohan Kadkol, Hyunjin Yoo, Jean-François Lalonde, James J. Clark
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We propose MergeableRF, a method for radiance field relighting through model parameter merging. Given multi-view images of a scene under known basis lights, our goal is to relight the scene by directly combining the parameters of radiance fields trained under these lights. This is motivated by the linearity of light transport: the image under a mixture of lights is the same weighted combination of the images under each light. However, combining the parameters of independently trained radiance fields does not produce the corresponding combination of images. To address this, we represent a scene with shared geometry and a separate appearance network for each basis light. We start these networks from the same initialization and introduce merge-aware training, which teaches their parameter combinations to reproduce the corresponding combinations of images. Given a target HDR environment map, we compute its basis-light coefficients and use them to merge the appearance networks into a single relit model. Our method supports both Neural Radiance Fields (NeRFs) and 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), without material estimation, additional relighting networks, or target-specific optimization. Unlike image-space blending, it requires only one rendering pass per viewpoint. Experiments on synthetic Neural Gaffer objects and real OLATverse captures demonstrate relighting quality competitive with methods trained on the same multi-light observations.



### Contrast Where It Matters: Visual Evidence-Guided Decoding for Mitigating Hallucinations in LVLMs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25524v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25524v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 05:26:32+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:26:32+00:00
- **Authors**: Yiheng Li, Wenhao Wang, Xingyi Li, Mingda Jia, Zichang Tan, Yinhang Tang, Zhen Lei, Yang Yang
- **Comment**: preprint
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Despite strong multimodal capabilities, large vision-language models (LVLMs) often hallucinate when linguistic priors outweigh visual evidence or when predictions are grounded in irrelevant or misleading image regions. Training-free contrastive decoding mitigates hallucinations by contrasting the original prediction with an auxiliary one that exposes linguistic priors. Yet existing methods focus on altering the auxiliary input or computation and do not ensure that either branch uses query-relevant visual evidence during decoding. We propose Visual Evidence-Guided Decoding (VEGD), a training-free framework that identifies query-relevant visual evidence and guides its use throughout contrastive decoding. VEGD locates this evidence by aggregating localization heads with strong visual responses and coherent spatial structure, then selectively weakens it in the auxiliary image. During generation, VEGD directs visual-carrier heads in both branches toward the same evidence location, strengthening access to intact evidence in the main branch while anchoring the auxiliary branch to its weakened counterpart. An adaptive distribution fusion strategy then combines or contrasts their predictions according to distribution divergence. Experiments across diverse LVLMs and benchmarks demonstrate that VEGD consistently reduces hallucinations.



### Support-Guided Triadic Decoding for Hallucination Mitigation in Multimodal Dialogue with LVLMs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25535v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25535v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 05:32:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:32:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Yiheng Li, Wenhao Wang, Xingyi Li, Zhilin Tang, Zichang Tan, Zhen Lei, Yang Yang
- **Comment**: preprint
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: In multimodal multi-turn dialogue, dialogue history plays a dual role: reliable responses provide useful context for subsequent turns, whereas hallucinated responses can propagate errors across turns. Existing training-free methods globally adjust the effect of dialogue history on generation, making it difficult to suppress misleading information without weakening useful context. To address this trade-off, we introduce Support-Guided Triadic Decoding (SGTD), a training-free framework that selectively controls the influence of unreliable history and fuses complementary predictions during decoding. Specifically, Historical Answer Support Estimation measures the visual support of historical responses against type-matched alternatives and identifies low-support content that may mislead later generation. Support-Targeted Attention Intervention then weakens the influence of this content while preserving the surrounding dialogue, producing a support-refined prediction. Triadic Decoding combines this prediction with the full-history prediction and answer-free visual evidence, while the Support-Adaptive Plausibility Constraint filters implausible tokens using visual agreement and historical support. Experiments on MMHalSnowball and VisDiaHalBench across representative LVLMs show that SGTD consistently achieves the highest accuracy under hallucinated histories while preserving the benefits of factual context.



### Query-Aware vs. Query-Agnostic Training-Free Video Token Pruning in Multimodal LLMs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25537v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25537v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 05:36:46+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:36:46+00:00
- **Authors**: Divya Kothandaraman, Timo Kumkel, Jaclyn Pytlarz
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video Large Language Models process large numbers of visual tokens, making token pruning important for efficient inference. Training-free pruning methods differ in whether and how they use the user query, and the value of query conditioning remains unclear. We present a controlled study of training-free, pre-LLM video token selection. Using a shared Qwen-3-VL backbone, token budgets, and decoding settings, we compare 21 pruning principles spanning query-agnostic, shallow query-conditioned, structured query-conditioned, and holistic strategies. To instantiate the last two categories, we introduce Structured Query Pruning, which decomposes the question into semantic anchors, and a Pareto variant, which combines anchor relevance with query-agnostic visual diversity. Across Video-MME, MVBench, and LongVideoBench, relevance computed from the raw question embedding is unreliable (8--16 points below the best methods), whereas anchors derived from query decomposition avoid this failure. However, query access is not inherently advantageous: strong query-agnostic coverage methods remain competitive or superior in the full-field comparison. Relative to a redundancy-only baseline, structured selection helps most on evidence-specific and long-context tasks at tight budgets; on broad tasks, the holistic selector improves over either relevance or redundancy alone. Further, pruning reduces cost and memory. These results support a practical design rule: preserve visual coverage by default, and add structured relevance when the task and token budget demand selective evidence retrieval.



### Anchored Inertial Proposals with Objective Verification for Diffusion-Prior Inverse Problems
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25551v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25551v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 05:55:24+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:55:24+00:00
- **Authors**: Minwoo Kim, Seunghyeok Shin, Dabin Kim, Hongki Lim
- **Comment**: Accepted to NeurIPS 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Training-free diffusion priors are powerful for inverse problems, but measurement guidance during reverse sampling is local: aggressive updates can improve immediate data fit while disrupting later denoising. We introduce an anchored inertial correction rule that separates proposal generation from acceptance. At each corrected step, the denoiser prediction anchors a frozen clean-space objective combining measurement fit with a noise-level-dependent proximal term. Heavy-ball dynamics with step size and momentum computed from the measured curvature generate a clean-state candidate; a separate verifier evaluates it with the same objective, with the denoiser prediction retained as fallback; both stages are derived from one objective for the whole trajectory, considered one step at a time. The accepted clean state is re-noised to continue sampling. The rule applies to pixel and latent diffusion with differentiable measurement operators, requires no retraining, and adds no denoiser calls inside the correction loop. We analyze how objective verification yields local nonincrease and displacement control. Across natural-image benchmarks, the method achieves competitive reconstruction quality. Code is available at https://github.com/Seunghyeok0715/PIVOT2026.



### TwoPass: Draft-Guided Few-Step Video Prediction for Robotic Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25553v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25553v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 05:58:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 05:58:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Yijun Liu, Shijia Ge, Tongtong Feng, Xin Wang, Yuan Meng, Yuwei Liu, Zhi Wang, Wenwu Zhu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Action-conditioned video prediction lets robots anticipate the visual consequences of their actions for planning and policy evaluation. Existing predictors denoise each future from noise without using their own estimate of it, which limits the quality of the predicted future, in particular of the manipulated objects, and requires many network calls. We present TwoPass, a self-conditioned training-and-inference framework that keeps the standard flow-matching objective. Self-Generated Draft Training supplies a detached clean-video estimate from the shared network as conditioning at the same noisy state. A Refinement-Only Late Adapter applies a dedicated residual transformation when a draft is present. At inference, Draft-Reuse Iterative Refinement obtains both a solver update and the next draft from each velocity prediction, requiring as few as 4 function evaluations (NFE) per video chunk. Training samples discrete solver-query noise levels and updates the pretrained backbone. Extensive experiments on Bridge and Language Table show that TwoPass achieves state-of-the-art results with 4 NFE, far fewer than the 50-59 calls of existing predictors. Code: https://github.com/PlantPotatoOnMoon/TwoPass.



### UniReFP: Robust Unified Fingerprinting for Vision Models against Cross-Task Repurposing Attacks
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25555v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25555v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 06:00:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 06:00:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziye Geng, Guang Yang, Yihang Chen, Lingfeng Yao, Xing Shi, Miao Pan, Xin Fu, Changqing Luo
- **Comment**: NeurIPS 2026 Spotlight
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We identify, for the first time, a new model modification attack--the cross-task model repurposing attack--that can render fingerprints generated by existing model fingerprinting approaches inapplicable. To address this challenge, we propose UniReFP--a novel unified model fingerprinting framework applicable to diverse vision models across different tasks--that utilizes ownership evidence in a shared class-level semantic space rather than original task-specific output space, enabling effective ownership verification when a protected model is repurposed across tasks with heterogeneous output formats. Specifically, UniReFP constructs a surrogate classifier by repurposing the protected model, enabling fingerprints to be generated independently of the original task-dependent output space of the protected model. Moreover, it optimizes grouped fingerprints to encode ownership evidence: fingerprints within the same group are encouraged to induce consistent semantic responses and intermediate representations on the surrogate classifier, while producing dispersed responses on independently trained reference classifiers.
