- **Authors**: Zinan Zheng, Nuo Chen, Jia Li
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision Language Models (VLMs) have shown remarkable capabilities in qualitative spatial reasoning, but remain limited in quantitative spatial reasoning. Current work leverages the VLM agent to invoke external vision tools to perform computation. However, these methods usually bind each functional requirement to a single predefined vision specialist, implicitly resulting in fixed and rigid tool pipelines. In practice, addressing diverse spatial scenarios and tasks demands a broader diversity in pipeline orchestration and vision specialist selection to achieve robust spatial reasoning. However, such diversity leads to a combinatorial execution space, making it challenging for the agent to orchestrate the optimal pipelines and specialists for different scenarios.   In this work, we propose SpatialMem, a memory framework that enables a VLM agent to accumulate tool-use experience and leverage that memory to orchestrate vision specialists to solve the spatial reasoning problem. SpatialMem builds a two-level memory structure, with task memory capturing tool orchestration and scene memory capturing tool selection. It further introduces a dual-state trajectory optimization process to preserve successful tool experiences and refine suboptimal ones. Moreover, existing spatial reasoning benchmarks suffer from quality issues and limited coverage of scene types and tasks. To address these gap, we propose MetricBench, a benchmark covering both indoor and outdoor scenes and three quantitative spatial reasoning tasks. Our method achieves state-of-the-art results on MetricBench, Q-Spatial, and SpatilRGPT-Bench, outperforming best VLM baselines by 32% on MetricBench. Additionally, the memory design yields a 9.1% absolute gain over a memory-free agent on MetricBench.



### Why Flat Drafts Stay Flat: Region-Mean Latents Govern Texture in Flow-Based Image Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25930v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25930v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 11:11:26+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 11:11:26+00:00
- **Authors**: Zihan Gu, Jiangcan Jia, Hua Zhang, Lulu Zhao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Flat color-block drafts specify layout and regional color while leaving interior detail unspecified, yet pretrained flow-based generators can preserve their flat appearance even under photorealistic prompts. We trace this behavior to a cross-scale mechanism linking fine pixel variation to regional means in variational autoencoder (VAE) latent space. Flattening photo regions systematically shifts their latent means, with offsets concentrated along a shared channel direction within each backbone. Controlled field exchanges in FLUX.1-dev and Qwen-Image establish the effect of this displacement on texture generation: adding the photo-derived regional mean field to a flat latent restores texture, whereas adding its complementary field alone largely preserves flatness. A second-order encoding expansion explains how encoder curvature can convert zero-mean perturbations into mean shifts. An analytically solvable conditional Gaussian flow shows how regional noise averaging enables near-unit endpoint sensitivity at high signal-to-noise ratios. This mechanism motivates training-free rendering through pixel dither before encoding, mean-field scaling, and early boundary holding. Paired low-rank adaptation (LoRA) with draft conditioning improves content recovery and produces photo-like regional means under the standard velocity objective. Explicit regional mean guidance remains effective after adaptation, continuously controlling texture and improving color fidelity. These findings connect encoding shifts to persistent generation behavior and support interventions evaluated for texture, spatial fidelity, and semantic recovery.



### AGSR: Aperture-Guided Super-Resolution for Gaussian Splatting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25934v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25934v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 11:20:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 11:20:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Ayman Alkhateeb, Hadi Amirpour, Christian Timmerer
- **Comment**: Accepted at the 2026 IEEE International Workshop on Multimedia Signal Processing (MMSP 2026). Recipient of the Best Paper Award
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables real-time novel view synthesis, but Gaussian rasterization attenuates fine-scale scene structure through the projected covariance of each primitive. We show that contracting these projected covariances at inference time can mitigate the effective bandwidth limitation induced by finite Gaussian footprints, motivating a multi-aperture rendering framework for super-resolution.   Based on this insight, we propose AGSR (Aperture-Guided Super-Resolution), a lightweight inference-time framework that progressively fuses multi-aperture renderings using uncertainty-guided feature aggregation and geometric conditioning from transmittance and depth cues. AGSR operates as a plug-in enhancement for pre-trained 3DGS models.   Experiments on the Mip-NeRF 360 benchmark show that AGSR outperforms lightweight image-space super-resolution baselines, with gains increasing as the underlying Gaussian representation deteriorates. The results provide evidence that covariance contraction exposes bandwidth-sensitive structural cues attenuated by native Gaussian rasterization. AGSR achieves these improvements while maintaining real-time performance with up to 139k parameters.



### TwinD: Generalizing Deepfake Detection from Blending to Entire Face Synthesis
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25935v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25935v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 11:20:39+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 11:20:39+00:00
- **Authors**: Andrii Yermakov, Jan Cech, Mario Fritz, Jiri Matas
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Facial deepfake detectors are usually trained on blending fakes, where a generated face is pasted back into a real frame. Recent generators synthesize the entire face and leave no traces of blending. On a benchmark of 19 datasets released from 2019 to 2026, the best prior facial deepfake detector reaches a mean AUROC of 96.2% on ten blending datasets, but none exceeds 78.1% on five datasets of entire face synthesis (EFS). AI-generated content (AIGC) detectors do not close this gap. We propose TwinD, a detector trained on FaceForensics++ fakes and two kinds of twins: fakes made from a real frame. A reconstruction twin is a real frame reconstructed by an autoencoder, with the real pixels put back in a random region. It targets the neural traces of a decoder. A blob twin blends an altered copy of a real frame with the frame itself, targeting blending artifacts. It needs no generator and no face-shaped mask. Together, these twins provide two complementary forensic cues that generalize to unseen manipulations. TwinDreaches a mean AUROC of 96.3%, 8.9 points above the prior state-of-the-art, with 98.1% on the blending datasets and 95.6% on the EFS datasets. Trained on twins alone, without a deepfake dataset, single models still reach 94.3% on average over three seeds. For reproducibility, the code, trained model, and training data are at: https://github.com/yermandy/TwinD



### Point Dynamics as Proxy for Robotic World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25946v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25946v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 11:31:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 11:31:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Dong An, Tianyu Liu, Haoran Ding, Wen Yang, Anqing Duan, Zhengzhong Liu, Eric P. Xing, Guangyi Liu
- **Comment**: 24 pages, 7 figures, 11 tables. Project page: https://marsaki.github.io/point-dynamics-proxy/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Robotic world models predict future video frames from the current observation and a sequence of robot actions. While recent models follow the given actions closely, manipulated objects often collapse or move inconsistently with the action. We attribute this to the conditioning: an action, or a rendering of the robot, specifies where the robot goes but not how the scene responds, leaving a single video model to infer the interaction dynamics implicitly while also rendering appearance. We therefore separate dynamics prediction from visual rendering, with 3D point dynamics as the proxy between them. Specifically, we lift the first frame into 3D points, simulate how they respond to the robot's motion with a learned point dynamics model, and render the simulated points back into pixel-aligned videos that any video model can read. In controlled experiments on the DROID dataset with two video renderers of different architectures, this point dynamics proxy reduces object motion error by 8.6% and 6.2% without degrading image quality, and by 8.1% on a real bimanual robot. Moreover, the video follows the simulation: more accurate dynamics yield more accurate object motion, and editing the points steers the video. This suggests that advances in dynamics modeling can translate into more faithful robotic world models.



### MorphTwin: Enriching Deformable Material Responses from Videos for Morphable and Interactive Physical Twins
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25963v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25963v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 11:51:16+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 11:51:16+00:00
- **Authors**: Haoqin Hong, Ding Fan, Fubin Dou, Tianyu Huang, Yuzhuo Chen, Zhemeng Zhang, Hanxiao Jiang, Congcong Zhu, Gerhard Petrus Hancke, Jingrun Chen, Rynson WH Lau
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reconstructing interactive physical twins from video requires accurately capturing how objects deform while maintaining efficient simulation. We observe that reproducing motion in a video does not guarantee correct responses to new forces when the physical representation restricts how an object can deform. In spring-based twins, coupling between volume and shape responses creates such a limitation. We present MorphTwin, which enriches local deformation modes to capture and independently control these responses while retaining standard spring forces and explicit simulation. To support interpretable material editing and transfer, we use offline calibration to map shared relative response changes to stiffness updates on each reconstructed graph. Under identical inverse settings on synthetic scenes, MorphTwin reduces the mean absolute error of Poisson's ratio by 75.8% and the mean relative error of Young's modulus by 22.1% compared to PhysTwin. We introduce an egocentric stereo and RGB-D benchmark covering interactions with objects of varying stiffness. Real-world experiments demonstrate improved reconstruction accuracy and up to 14.4% lower future tracking error than PhysTwin at interactive speeds.



### Sample-efficient end-to-end Optical Music Recognition through Active Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25975v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25975v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 11:59:52+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 11:59:52+00:00
- **Authors**: Eric Ayllon, Francisco J. Castellanos, Antonio Rios-Vila
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: End-to-end approaches to Optical Music Recognition (OMR) have recently shown promising results, but their performance often degrades on unseen data. A common solution is to pretrain and fine-tune models with annotated samples from the target domain, improving adaptation to new distributions. However, this process typically requires extensive manual annotation to yield competitive models, making it costly and time-consuming. In this work, we explore Active Learning to reduce this burden by selecting informative samples for labeling in low-resource scenarios, where the amount of manual annotation that can be performed is limited. We evaluate both uncertainty- and diversity-based sampling strategies on staff-level and full-page corpora. Results show that confidence-based sampling performs comparably to oracle selection (based on true error), and that each scenario benefits from a tailored strategy.



### Multi-View Transformer with Geometry-Guided Multi-Modal ControlNet for 3D Virtual Try-On
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25979v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25979v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 12:03:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 12:03:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Sarthak Batra, Armin Mustafa, Adrian Hilton
- **Comment**: 20 pages, 2 figures, 3 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Editing a 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) capture of a person so that they wear a new garment requires every rendered view to agree on the same garment details. Existing 3D virtual try-on (VTON) pipelines, however, edit training views largely independently and fuse them with hand-designed heuristics such as manually chosen reference views and fixed attention-blending weights. The resulting inconsistencies are baked into the 3D representation and surface as texture drift, blurred prints, and implausible geometry in novel views. We present GS-VTON-MVT, a three-stage framework that combines implicit and explicit 3D reasoning for consistent 3DGS try-on. (i) A multi-view inpainting transformer jointly edits a set of training views with full cross-view self-attention, so that cross-view correspondence is learned rather than imposed. (ii) A geometry-guided multi-modal ControlNet conditions the diffusion editor on the garment image together with depth and surface normals rendered from the current 3DGS state, supplying explicit structure where visual evidence is weakest. (iii) A pose-adaptive persona-aware attention replaces the fixed blending weight between current-view and reference-view features with weights derived from camera-pose proximity. On the 3D-VTONBench benchmark, GS-VTON-MVT improves FID, KID, LPIPS-based cross-view consistency, and user preference over general-purpose 3D editors and prior 3D try-on methods.



### Learning What Actions Change: Action Effect Matching for Dynamics-Faithful World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25980v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25980v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 12:03:05+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 12:03:05+00:00
- **Authors**: John Won, Yonghoon Dong, Jinwoo Shin
- **Comment**: 27 pages, preprint
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World models aim to predict environment dynamics under actions, enabling the prediction of action consequences without executing them in the real world. Yet despite producing increasingly realistic videos, current world models often fail to capture how small action changes affect future observations. This limits their use in tasks such as robotic policy evaluation and model-based planning, where nearly identical actions can lead to qualitatively different outcomes. To address this, we propose AFFECT, an auxiliary training objective for learning the distribution of visual changes induced by action perturbations. Specifically, we inject temporally correlated noise into the actions of a policy trained on task-agnostic human teleoperation. We then train the world model so that its generated videos respond to this noise as the recorded videos do, without requiring repeated executions from the same initial state. Across OGBench simulation and real-world DROID and ALOHA-2 setups, AFFECT makes world-model rollouts more faithful to real outcomes. On DROID, the Pearson correlation between policy success rates in the world model and on the real robot rises from 0.47 to 0.79, alongside up to 23% lower FVD. These gains carry over to planning, where selecting actions with AFFECT-trained world models improves task success by up to 8.3%.



### Fixing the Twin, Not the Detector: Sim-to-Real Roadside LiDAR Detection from Digital-Twin Data
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26035v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26035v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 12:45:33+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 12:45:33+00:00
- **Authors**: KuanLin Chen, Feng-jui Kuo
- **Comment**: 8 pages, 1 figure, 5 tables. Accepted to the IEEE BigData 2026 Cup (UCF UrbanTwin Sim2Real LiDAR Challenge); first place, LUMPI track. Code: https://github.com/dipper13579/urbantwin-sim2real-lidar
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Roadside LiDAR detectors are expensive to train on real labels, and digital twins promise to replace these labels, but a detector trained on a twin meets a different sensor, different objects and different annotation conventions on the real site. We describe the pipeline that placed first in the LUMPI track of the UrbanTwin Sim2Real LiDAR Challenge (IEEE BigData 2026 Cup, 0.5541 combined score) and fifth on its V2X-Real track (0.8102). Every detector is a CenterPoint model trained only on synthetic data, on a single 8 GB laptop GPU. Most of the gain came from correcting differences between the twin and the real site rather than from the detector. We align the twin's coordinates, point density and box convention with the test sensor, generate the pedestrians and riders that the twins lack, plus street clutter as unlabeled negatives, and rebuild twin objects at real size and real point density. A non-learned resampling to the per-cell point density of unlabeled real frames lifts LUMPI realism from 0.589 to 0.947, against 0.949 between real frames. Label-free post-processing then exploits what a fixed roadside sensor guarantees: static structures recur in every frame, and real objects have characteristic point statistics. Reporting the validation and test gain of each component side by side, we find that corrections of systematic sim-to-real differences transfer to the test set, typically keeping a quarter to three quarters of their validation gain, while choices that fit the few objects of a validation set do not.



### Streaming-AAA: Streaming AAA Game Video Generation at High Resolution, High Frame Rate, and Low Latency
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26040v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26040v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 12:50:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-20 06:53:50+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhihao Hu, Jianqi Ma, Jun Zhang, Jiayang Bai, Haifeng Shi, Yanxiang Zhuang, Zhongqian Sun, Weidong Zhang, Wei Yang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Controllable streaming video generation offers a promising route toward interactive game simulation, yet existing methods remain far from practical gameplay. They often fail to jointly achieve high visual fidelity, high frame rates, and low interaction latency, resulting in poor responsiveness and degraded user experience. We present Streaming-AAA, a new paradigm for controllable infinite generation of AAA-quality game videos. Our system generates 1080p gameplay videos at 43 FPS with 198 ms end-to-end latency interaction on merely NVIDIA A800 GPUs, while supporting long-horizon play without severe temporal degradation or collapse. Streaming-AAA is built upon three key designs. First, we redesign the VAE architecture for efficient high-resolution decoding, enabling real-time synthesis at 1080p. Second, we pretrain the diffusion model using diffusion forcing and significantly extend the temporal training horizon, allowing the model to capture long-term dependencies and maintain persistent memory. Third, we perform post-training distillation and memory compression to improve inference efficiency while retaining visual quality and temporal consistency. Experiments show that Streaming-AAA substantially improves visual fidelity, frame rate, latency, and long-term stability over prior controllable video generation approaches, enabling a significantly more playable experience for interactive AAA game video generation.



### PaveSplat: Resolution-Robust Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting with Residually Corrected Surface-Aligned Covariances
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26045v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26045v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 12:55:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 12:55:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Janghun Kim, Hyun-Cheol Kim, Eunbin An, Myeonghun Oh, Hyon-Gon Choo
- **Comment**: Accepted to ACCV 2026. 16 pages, 4 figures, 4 tables. Project page: https://janghunn.github.io/pavesplat/ ; code: https://github.com/janghunn/pavesplat
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We present PaveSplat, a feed-forward Gaussian splatting method that adds a learned residual to the analytic surface-aligned covariance, keeping surfels paving the visible surface across rendering resolutions. Pixel-aligned feed-forward Gaussian splatting predicts one primitive per pixel from a few images in a single pass, and its rendering quality depends on how well these primitives cover the surface. Recent surfel-based methods compute the surface-aligned covariance analytically from predicted depth. However, this covariance has no way to correct the surfel orientation where the depth-derived frame is ill-posed. The surfels then cover the surface loosely; at the training resolution each surfel's one-pixel minimum footprint covers the gaps, but they show up as holes when the same prediction is re-rendered at a higher resolution. To address this, we keep the analytic covariance as an initialization, with its axes aligned to the surface tangents, and add an identity-initialized unit-quaternion residual that reorients a surfel only where this improves rendering, turning surfels that the analytic frame mis-aims back onto the surface. On RealEstate10K, PaveSplat achieves the best PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS among single-Gaussian-per-pixel methods at every scale (1x-8x). The native-resolution gain is small; the benefit appears when the same prediction is rendered at higher display resolutions without re-running the network. On zero-shot DL3DV, it leads PSNR within this group at every scale and ranks first or second on SSIM/LPIPS. A coverage-hole analysis shows fewer uncovered pixels than both baselines under high-resolution re-rendering.



### Less Than Meets the Eye: Action-Set Identifiability and Principled Abstention for 3D Physical Geometry
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26056v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26056v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:01:26+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:01:26+00:00
- **Authors**: Reshad Ul Karim, Abrar Samin, Sammam Mahdi, Syeda Maliha Tabassum, Md Rashad Al Hasan Rony
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A corridor, its mirror reflection, and its image on a display can look identical, yet their physical surfaces lie at different depths. We test whether bounded camera motions distinguish these explanations. Intervene3D takes an image and an initial geometry estimate, predicts the view under each hypothesis and candidate motion, and selects a motion by maximin separability. It reports the mechanism and first physical surface only if the available motions separate the hypotheses still in contention. Otherwise it abstains. An adequacy test also rejects observations that no hypothesis explains. On a matched synthetic benchmark with exact landmarks, disabling abstention gives confident answers on 76.4% of unresolvable scenes. With abstention, none receives a confident answer under exact or noisy landmarks. At 100% accuracy on answered scenes, maximin selection answers 61.0% of scenes and summed selection 43.1%. The adequacy test lowers wrong answers on strongly curved mirror scenes from 56.8% to 12.4%. Real display experiments compare two hypotheses using one frozen depth-network estimate. The second image supplies photometric evidence and is never passed through the depth network. One executed motion reduces the pooled median surface error of twelve networks by 31.5%. These networks have larger error inside the annotated panel than outside on 33.4% of illusion-benchmark images. They place the first physical surface behind the glass on 36.9% of Mirror3D frames.



### One-to-Many: Diverse and Bidirectional Image-to-Image Translation via Rectified Flow
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26058v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26058v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:02:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:02:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Sho Shimotsumagari, Seiichi Uchida, Shumpei Takezaki
- **Comment**: 12 pages, 13 figures, 4 tables. Includes supplementary material
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Many paired image-to-image translation tasks admit multiple valid target appearances, while training provides only one target per source. We propose One-to-Many Flow (O2MFlow), a latent-conditioned Rectified Flow (RF) model for one-to-many generation and backward translation (target$\rightarrow$source) within a single transport framework. Conditioning the RF dynamics on an appearance latent turns transport into a family of latent-indexed bijections: a fixed latent gives a mapping that is inverted by time-reversed integration, and sampled latents produce diverse outputs for the same input. We formalize reusable appearance variations as Shared Latent Transport and show that Kullback--Leibler (KL) regularization directly controls the source--latent distribution gap between training and generation. Across three benchmarks, O2MFlow offers dataset-dependent forward diversity--fidelity trade-offs and the lowest backward LPIPS-Dist among evaluated methods, with the lowest backward FID on two benchmarks.



### Domain-Aware Conformal Prediction for Trustworthy Spacecraft Pose Estimation under Sim-to-Real Shift
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26065v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26065v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:08:51+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:08:51+00:00
- **Authors**: Nidhal Eddine Chenni, Arunkumar Rathinam, Djamila Aouada
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Autonomous rendezvous and proximity operations require not only an accurate estimate of the pose of the target spacecraft, but also a reliable indication of when this estimate can be trusted. Conformal prediction provides such an indication with a formal guarantee, by producing pose uncertainty sets that contain the true pose with a specified probability. Such a guarantee, however, assumes that calibration and test data follow the same distribution, an assumption that is violated in orbital perception, where labelled calibration images are available only from synthetic renderings while operational images are real. In this work, we show on the SPEED+ dataset that conformal pose estimation calibrated on synthetic images loses its coverage guarantee on real images, and that many of the resulting failures are silent: the predicted pose set appears valid but excludes the true pose. To address this, we calibrate on synthetic images translated to the appearance of real images. Since translation alters appearance while preserving geometry, the synthetic pose labels remain valid and no labelled real images are required. Experiments with two translation models show that this calibration restores coverage above the specified level for miscoverage levels of 0.1 and above, and reduces silent failures from up to 43.5% to at most 3.3% of the test images, bringing conformal pose estimation a step closer to trustworthy deployment in space.



### DfD-GS: Defocus Image Prior for Metric-Scale Single-View Depth Estimation and View Synthesis
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26072v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26072v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:14:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:14:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Shiori Ueda, Rinto Yagawa, Hideo Saito, Shohei Mori
- **Comment**: Project page: https://sh1027.github.io/dfd-gs/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recovering metric-scale geometry from observations at a single viewpoint remains fundamentally ill-posed, limiting the reliability of novel view synthesis. Recent methods rely on learned monocular depth priors, which provide strong structural estimates but can suffer from scale ambiguity and dataset-dependent biases. We propose DfD-GS, a feed-forward framework that predicts a metric-scale 3D Gaussian scene representation from a single-view all-in-focus--defocused image pair. The key idea is to exploit defocus blur as a physical metric depth cue. DfD-GS first estimates a defocus-based metric disparity map from the all-in-focus--defocused image pair and the corresponding defocus strength. This estimate is used to align and refine a monocular depth prior to metric scale. Visible and hidden 3D Gaussians are then predicted for occlusion-aware novel view synthesis. Experiments on synthetic-defocus datasets constructed from Booster and Middlebury show that DfD-GS outperforms single-image baselines in metric depth estimation and novel view synthesis. On WildRGB-D, DfD-GS achieves lower AbsRel and LPIPS but higher depth RMSE than the single-image baselines. Additional analyses show that DfD-GS maintains a more stable metric scale under field-of-view changes. Qualitative results on a real dataset show recovery of the main scene structure under optical defocus.



### Frequency-Synchronized Boundary Coupling for Training-Free Multi-Prompt Long Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26098v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26098v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:33:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:33:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Yipan Xu, Shuyong Gao, Jiyuan Fu, Keliang Yin, Jiayu Chen, Qianyu Guo, Yang Liu, Wenqiang Zhang
- **Comment**: Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://yipanxu-22.github.io/FreqSync-project
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Text-to-video generation has made remarkable progress with diffusion models and transformer-based video architectures, yet generating coherent long videos from evolving textual descriptions remains challenging. Training long-video models from scratch requires substantial computation and large-scale multi-event data, while training-free multi-prompt generation must activate new prompt semantics without breaking temporal continuity. We identify a frequency-agnostic boundary coupling problem in existing transition strategies: weak coupling causes abrupt changes, flicker, and background jumps, whereas overly strong or coarse coupling may over-preserve source semantics and produce ghosting or duplicated subjects around prompt boundaries. To address this problem, we propose FreqSync, a training-free framework that formulates prompt transitions as frequency-selective boundary coupling. FreqSync combines Frequency-Synchronized Source-Conditioned Attention (FS-SCA) with Residual-Clipped Spectral Stitching (RCSS), enabling adjacent segments to share stable low- and mid-frequency structure and motion cues while keeping high-frequency prompt-specific details target-dominant under explicit residual constraints. Extensive experiments show that FreqSync achieves state-of-the-art transition smoothness and prompt alignment while maintaining strong perceptual video quality, demonstrating its effectiveness for coherent multi-prompt long video generation.



### UniVEP: Unifying Velocity and Endpoint Prediction for Robot Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26103v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26103v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:36:23+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:36:23+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhengtu Liang, Qiwei Liang, Tao Lin, Shihang Weng, Boyang Cai, Xiangyu Chen, Yikeng Chen, Jiaming Liang, Bohan Tan, Yu Yan, Renjing Xu
- **Comment**: 43 pages, 8 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Flow-matching robot policies usually use one prediction target throughout action generation, even though recovering actions from noise and polishing nearly clean actions bring different challenges. We systematically compare velocity prediction (V), endpoint prediction (X), and velocity-weighted endpoint prediction (X--V) across multiple robot policies, benchmarks, and inference budgets. On StarVLA and LiLa-WAM, X--V works best for few-step generation, while V gains more from iterative refinement. We further uncover their complementary strengths across noise levels: clean actions have concentrated covariance spectra, endpoint predictions stay more stable under high noise, and velocity predictions offer more accurate corrections near clean actions. Inspired by these findings, we introduce UniVEP, which learns both prediction targets together with consistency supervision and switches from endpoint recovery to velocity refinement as noise drops. Each step only needs one network run, and joint training also boosts single-step endpoint generation. Tests on simulated and real-world manipulation show better success rates and efficiency. On RoboTwin, UniVEP hits 61.36% success with one StarVLA run, beating the best single-target baseline at sixteen runs (59.96%). With LiLa-WAM, two runs outperform the best sixteen-step single-target baseline and cut inference latency by 77.1%.



### HiUni: Harmonizing Understanding and Generation in Shared Visual Space
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26107v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26107v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:37:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:37:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiahao Hu, Jingdong Du, Yichen Zhang, Yi Song, Zhenyu Zhang, Jiaxin Niu, Ruihang Li, Xiangzhao Hao, Yiqian Zhang, Yong Huang, Zefeng Zhang, Zheyuan Gu, Shuohuan Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Unified multimodal models aim to support visual understanding and generation within a single framework. However, prevailing approaches often separate their visual representations or backbone computation to accommodate the different requirements of these two tasks, which limits the degree of unification. In this paper, we present HiUni, which revisits this design choice and explores whether a pretrained vision language model can be directly extended into a unified model with extensive parameter sharing. Specifically, HiUni reuses the same language-aligned ViT and dense Transformer backbone for both capabilities, while confining task-specific computation to the output side. Yet such a design introduces two key challenges. Generation in pixel space is difficult to optimize, and semantic abstraction in pretrained ViT discards fine spatial details required for synthesis. To address these challenges, HiUni introduces a \emph{Pixel Diffusion Head} tailored to direct pixel-space optimization and a lightweight \emph{HiGate} pathway that provides fine-grained spatial details near the output stage. Extensive experiments show that HiUni achieves a similar average understanding score to the pretrained VLM while attaining competitive performance on image generation and editing. Controlled comparisons and analyses further demonstrate that a single dense backbone can effectively support both capabilities without task-specific backbone computation, highlighting a simpler path toward unified multimodal modeling.



### Learning Reconstruction Errors as Spatiotemporal Evidence for AI-Generated Video Detection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26116v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26116v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:45:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:45:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiahua Jiang, Jinglai Li, Jianru Zhang, Jin Zhu
- **Comment**: 22 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The proliferation of AI-generated videos has raised growing concerns about misinformation, fraud, and potential societal risks, creating an urgent need for detection. Reconstruction-error-based methods have shown strong empirical performance for AI-generated image detection by exploiting discrepancies between an input and its reconstructed version. Their extension to video detection, however, faces two challenges: (i) a fixed reconstructor is not optimized for the detection objective and may yield weak discriminative signals; and (ii) an additional raw-video feature stream is often introduced to model temporal dependencies, increasing architectural overhead. To address these challenges, we develop a detector that learns and exploits reconstruction errors as a unified source of spatiotemporal evidence. First, we jointly optimize a reconstructor and the downstream classifier to produce reconstruction errors that are more informative for detecting AI-generated videos. Second, we show that consecutive reconstruction errors alone carry temporal evidence, and thus a 3D convolution over them captures spatiotemporal evidence without a raw-video stream. Extensive experiments on over 30 existing AI-video generators demonstrate competitive performance against baselines.



### Better Retrieval, Not Better Binding: Pretraining Exposure in Egocentric Video Retrieval
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26120v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26120v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:46:57+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:46:57+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuqi Guo, Michael Wray
- **Comment**: 17 pages, 4 figures, 3 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video-language models (VLMs) achieve strong performance on video-text retrieval and are increasingly used for egocentric video understanding. Yet, despite gains in zero-shot retrieval, they have shown limitations in compositional understanding, and it remains unclear whether they understand compositional relationships between verbs and nouns. In this work, we introduce Ego-ExBind, a compositional diagnostic protocol for analyzing pretraining exposure and verb-noun binding in egocentric video-text retrieval. Ego-ExBind consists of an Exposure Ledger built from the EgoClip pretraining corpus used by EgoVLPv2, which defines Seen Composition, Unseen Composition, and Unseen Atom splits for EPIC-KITCHENS-100 multi-instance retrieval (MIR). We further probe noun and verb binding channels through controlled noun-swap and verb-swap negatives. We find that zero-shot retrieval is positively associated with pair exposure within seen compositions, even after controlling for the marginal frequencies of verbs and nouns. However, higher pair exposure is associated with larger noun-swap margins and slightly smaller verb-swap margins. This asymmetry motivates us to test whether exposure reweighting can improve retrieval with little change in the measured swap margins. To isolate the effect of exposure input, we keep the trained checkpoint fixed and replace only the exposure input at inference. We demonstrate that this exposure reweighting can improve video-text retrieval without stronger verb-noun compositional understanding, and that the gain depends on the correct sample-level exposure correspondence. By separating exposure-driven retrieval gains from verb-noun binding, Ego-ExBind provides a diagnostic framework for evaluating whether future improvements in egocentric MIR reflect compositional understanding rather than pretraining familiarity.



### CryoGaussian: Heterogeneous Cryo-EM Reconstruction Made Explicit with Gaussian Splatting
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26128v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26128v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 13:51:14+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 13:51:14+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziwen Wang, Jiajun Fan, Chaoran Cheng, Ruihan Guo, Yanru Qu, Juexiao Zhou, Ge Liu
- **Comment**: 59 pages, 25 figures, 15 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Single-particle cryo-electron microscopy (cryo-EM) determines macromolecular structures from noisy particle images. However, rare, transient compositional states and subtle, continuous conformational variation are difficult to resolve. We present CryoGaussian, a Gaussian splatting method for heterogeneous cryo-EM reconstruction that fits an explicit Gaussian density representation directly to the particle images through a differentiable rasterizer rendering orthographic line integrals modulated by the contrast transfer function. The Gaussians are initialized randomly, with their number derived from the estimated spatial support of the molecular density and their minimum size bounded by the Nyquist frequency of the particle images. To capture the compositional and conformational variation across particle images, we learn particle-image and per-Gaussian latents that jointly modulate the base position, amplitude, and scale of each Gaussian. Each structural state is then reconstructed by fitting the Gaussians conditioned on its inferred latent. On CryoBench, CryoGaussian achieves state-of-the-art reconstruction accuracy for both compositional and conformational heterogeneity. On experimental data, it resolves a rare compositional state of the 50S ribosomal subunit (class C4, 1.3% of particles) with rRNA helix 68 docked in the absence of the central protuberance, and continuous conformational motion of the SF3b and helicase subcomplexes of the pre-catalytic spliceosome. We further optimize the rasterizer for additive cryo-EM projection, reducing training time by 12.8-28.0$\times$.



### Can World Models Predict Rice Growth? Toward Agricultural World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26145v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26145v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:02:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:02:03+00:00
- **Authors**: Wenkai Ji, Zeteng Li, Yinkun Guo, Jie Zhou, Liang He, Zhenong Jin
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: World models can imagine how a physical scene evolves over the next few seconds, but growers need to know how a crop will develop over weeks to months: will this rice plant have headed a month from now, and how will it look at harvest? We introduce agricultural world modeling, the task of predicting how the same plant will look at a requested horizon of days to months, from one photo of it, its cultivar, and its camera view. In preliminary analysis, even state-of-the-art image generators get this wrong: they often grow the plant too little or give it the color and form of the wrong growth stage. We argue that they do not work out the plant's developmental state, which the photo reveals only indirectly. We therefore propose AgroWM, which reasons about development before it draws growth. Its reasoning module, PhenoClock, works out the current growth stage, predicts the future one, and writes a Growth Script with numeric targets for height, plant area, and color; its generation module, the Rooted Renderer, then edits the photo to follow this script, adding new organs and colors to the photographed plant. Because pixel scores can prefer an unchanged copy of the photo, we instead check each forecast's traits and developmental timing against the plant's own later photos. On rice, 54.6% of the forecasts of AgroWM are matched to the target stage (Stage R@1), against 34.8% for GPT Image 2.5, 24.0% for Qwen Image 2.1, and 47.4% for the strongest domain-specific baseline, the same backbone fine-tuned without Growth Scripts; 9 to 20 weeks ahead, it still reaches 46.4%, against 35.7% for that baseline. On maize, a crop with a different body plan and stage scale, the same design again scores best on all four measures.



### A Practical Proprioceptive Data Collection Utility for the Unitree GO1
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26146v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26146v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:02:25+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:02:25+00:00
- **Authors**: Jack Anders Smitterberg, Tan Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: The Unitree Go1 is a widely used platform for legged robotics research, yet collecting proprioceptive data from it is unnecessarily difficult: the Unitree SDK is sparsely documented, and ROS1 (Robot Operating System), the Go1's native middleware, is fully deprecated. We present an open-source graphical utility that enables researchers to reliably record proprioceptive data from the Go1, including joint states, IMU (Inertial Measurement Unit) readings, foot force sensor data, and gyroscope measurements, without requiring ROS expertise. The tool provides a simple GUI (Graphical User Interface) for starting, stopping, and labeling recordings in real time. The contribution of this paper is the collection utility. We characterize the acquisition quality of 51 recordings collected with it, including the achieved sampling rate and repeated samples. As a secondary, deliberately modest check that the data is useful, we train a terrain classifier on it. The results are weak: a Temporal Convolutional Network reaches about 38% accuracy on held-out recordings across ten terrain classes (10% would be chance), and a window-level split that ignores recording boundaries inflates the same measurement to about 99%. We report this as a demonstration that the logged signals carry terrain information, not as a competitive classifier. The utility is designed to lower the barrier to collecting Go1 proprioceptive data without ROS.



### When Foundation Models Disagree: Representational Heterogeneity for Calibrated Uncertainty in Whole-Slide Image Classification
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26159v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, q-bio.QM
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26159v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:15:07+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:15:07+00:00
- **Authors**: Yekai Shen, Yuqian Wang, Zishun Liao, Rongsheng Wang, Wenting Chen, S. Kevin Zhou, Qingsong Yao
- **Comment**: accepted by IEEE BIBM 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Pathology foundation models supply strong representations for whole-slide image classification, yet the confidence attached to their predictions remains hard to trust. Prevailing uncertainty estimators act within a single feature space and thereby overlook the uncertainty that the choice of foundation-model representation itself induces. This work introduces CALIFUSE, a calibration-oriented framework that fuses several pathology foundation models for the specific purpose of uncertainty estimation. Departing from accuracy-driven fusion, CALIFUSE reads representational heterogeneity across encoders as an explicit confidence cue. Slide-level decision consensus is coupled with chunk-level evidence agreement quantified through Jensen-Shannon divergence, so that both prediction stability and fine-grained evidence consistency inform the estimate. Foundation-model encoders stay frozen. A task-specific nnMIL classifier is still trained for each FM; CALIFUSE then estimates confidence at inference without training an extra fusion module. Evaluated on seven WSI classification tasks under several foundation-model combinations, the framework sharpens calibration, misclassification detection, and selective prediction relative to both the strongest single-encoder baseline and naive ensemble averaging, all while preserving competitive balanced accuracy.



### MultiBinder: Preventing Multi-Subject Identity Leakage in Personalized Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26164v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26164v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:18:00+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:18:00+00:00
- **Authors**: Wei Li, Yiheng Zhang, Zhaofan Qiu, Ting Yao, Xiaoyan Sun, Tao Mei
- **Comment**: ACM Multimedia 2026
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Personalized video generation faces a significant scalability challenge: as the number of custom subjects increases, existing methods suffer from severe identity leakage and semantic entanglement, resulting in videos with blended attributes and incoherent narratives. We identify that this stems from a lack of explicit binding between textual entity phrases and their corresponding visual patterns prior to fusion in diffusion models. To address this, we propose MultiBinder, a novel framework built on a ``bind-then-narrate'' paradigm. Our approach introduces a two-stage conditioning process before video diffusion. First, Cross-modal Entity Binding devises an entity-aware Q-Former to extract identity-centric features from each subject's reference image and tightly align them with its unique entity phrase, yielding disentangled ``identity tokens.'' Second, a Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) acts as a ``narrative director'' in the Narrative-Aware Subject Arrangement stage, capturing the action of each subject from the text prompt and synthesizes contextualized subject features that encapsulate ``who is doing what'' and preserve role-specific distinctions. These features are then injected into a video diffusion model via dedicated conditioning pathways, ensuring precise attribute allocation. Extensive experiments on both general and newly curated multi-subject benchmarks show that MultiBinder significantly outperforms existing methods in multi-subject identity fidelity.



### StreamEgo: Streaming Exocentric-to-Egocentric Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26165v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26165v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:18:24+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:18:24+00:00
- **Authors**: Tianyi Xu, Boyang Lyu, Yunkai Tang, Kairan Ding, Jiahang Li, Weiyu Guo, Yandong Guo, Shuchen Weng, Boxin Shi
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Exocentric-to-egocentric (Exo2Ego) video generation transforms third-person recordings into first-person viewpoints, unlocking immersive AR/VR experiences and scalable demonstration synthesis for embodied robotics. Extending it to a streaming setting is expected to further enable live applications. However, existing methods suffer from degraded manipulation regions, overfitting and confinement to fixed-length clips with prohibitive latency. To address these limitations, we present StreamEgo, a real-time framework for unbounded, generalizable Exo2Ego video generation. To resolve intricate manipulation dynamics while supporting the streaming pipeline, a streaming dual-branch prior provides pixel-aligned guidance for translating appearance and skeletal kinematics. We also introduce a region-aware loss curriculum to improve generalization and stabilize multi-objective diffusion training. Furthermore, we distill the model into a chunk-wise autoregressive model, augmented with a region-aware corruption mechanism and a first-chunk curriculum, enabling high-quality, generalizable real-time streaming Exo2Ego generation at 24.3 FPS on a single H100 GPU.



### SpatialScout-V2: Can VLM Agents Actively Explore Real-World 3D Scenes and Localize Objects?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26177v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26177v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:28:18+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:28:18+00:00
- **Authors**: Zhaoxi Zhang, Hao Liang, Linxuan Wu, Yiming Xu, Yifu Yuan, Kun Liang, Chenming Tang, Meiyi Qiang, Wentao Zhang, Weikang Li, Yunfang Wu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Spatial intelligence requires agents to acquire visual evidence and integrate partial observations into coherent three-dimensional representations. We introduce SpatialScout-V2, which extends SpatialScout's active mapping task from simulation to real residential scenes through three advances: real visual observations, preservation of original scene content, and interactive exploration of recorded views. Built from 100 homes in Realsee3D, the benchmark covers 3,171 target objects across 29 categories. Given a fixed target inventory, agents autonomously select recorded viewpoints and viewing directions, integrate calibrated RGB observations, and iteratively estimate and revise object centers within a fixed interaction budget. We evaluate the localization accuracy and completeness of object-center maps using mean IoU, IoU-threshold hit rates, and target coverage. Evaluating seven frontier vision-language models (VLMs), we find that six achieve an all-target mean IoU below 20\%. Even the best-performing model, Claude Opus 5.5, reaches only 22.52\%, with just 18.36\% of targets exceeding an IoU of 0.5. Further analysis shows that broad submission coverage can coexist with limited localization accuracy, while target omissions further reduce overall scores. These results highlight substantial challenges in constructing accurate and complete object-center maps through active exploration of real scenes.



### FlexVSR: Continuous-Scale Super-Resolution for Streaming Video Generation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26178v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26178v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:29:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:29:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Ji Wang, Haonan An, Jiasheng Zhang, Renlong Chen
- **Comment**: 23 pages, 5 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Real-time streaming video generation has gained rapid momentum in live content creation and interactive media. Deploying such systems in practice requires flexible scaling factors to accommodate diverse source resolutions, target displays, and computational budgets. However, extending fixed-scale generative video super-resolution (VSR) to varying target scales poses two challenges: downsampling from a larger fixed scale incurs redundant cost, while re-optimizing the diffusion transformer (DiT) is costly and alters the shared generative prior. To address both, we propose FlexVSR, a decoupled framework for continuous-scale streaming generative VSR with supervision at only a few scales. Our key insight is that naively resampling fixed-scale latents onto a new target grid induces scale-dependent input mismatch, which can be effectively addressed through parameter-efficient, scale-aware adaptation of the latent interface without updating the DiT. To this end, FlexVSR introduces ReLSA (Refined Latent Scale Adaptation), a lightweight latent adapter that constructs and refines target-grid latents before they enter the DiT, enabling a shared frozen VSR prior to generalize across continuous scales with only 0.274M trainable parameters and an adaptation cost of 7.3 H100 GPU-hours, while introducing no additional temporal dependencies and preserving the native causal streaming protocol. Extensive experiments demonstrate competitive or superior reconstruction and temporal quality, lower latency than most native-scale streaming baselines and offline methods, and effective generalization to unseen continuous scales, filling the continuous-scale gap in real-time streaming generative VSR.



### RegionAgent: Two-Stage Preference Optimization for Regional Image Restoration
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26179v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26179v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:29:56+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:29:56+00:00
- **Authors**: Weiyu Zhou, Jiacong Tang, Tao Hu, Axi Niu, Jinqiu Sun, Peng Wu, Wei Dong, Qingsen Yan
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Images under non-uniform coupled degradations require diverse restoration capabilities for different degraded regions. Existing methods employ globally uniform operations, potentially overlooking regional differences and causing incomplete restoration or even damage to image content. Moreover, a degraded region may require successive restoration actions, and earlier actions may alter residual degradation characteristics or introduce artifacts, potentially hindering subsequent restoration. To address these challenges, we propose RegionAgent, an adaptive restoration framework that trains a Vision-Language Model (VLM) through two-stage preference learning to generate a region-specific restoration action at each iteration. Specifically, we first train the VLM to select an action suited to the current region through step-level Direct Preference Optimization (DPO), using preferences derived from immediate restoration feedback to supervise only the differing component in each action pair. To account for the effects of current actions on subsequent restoration, we then apply trajectory-level DPO, using preferences derived from complete trajectory scores to supervise the sole differing action in each trajectory pair. Furthermore, as conventional image-pair datasets do not directly provide preferences over regional restoration decisions, we construct a regional restoration preference dataset comprising 10K action pairs and 20K trajectory pairs for two-stage VLM training. Experiments across multiple datasets demonstrate strong restoration performance under both mixed and single degradations. Ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of two-stage preference learning in improving restoration quality.



### Invertible Transport Between Sample-Only Distributions via Projected Characteristic Functions
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26187v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26187v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:36:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:36:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Juan Gutiérrez, Victor Gutiérrez-García, José Luis Blanco-Murillo
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Many useful latent distributions can be sampled but do not have a tractable density. Such distributions may be represented by empirical collections of real-world observations or generated by scientific simulators and black-box generative models. These sample-only priors cannot be used directly in likelihood-based normalizing flows, which require evaluating the prior density. We propose Projected Characteristic-Function matching (PCF), a density-free framework for learning an invertible map between a data distribution and a prior specified only through samples.   Our method aligns distributions using projected empirical characteristic functions, eliminating the need for density estimation, adversarial training, or paired observations. To address the non-uniqueness of the transport map, we introduce an optional transport regularizer that selects lower-displacement transformations while preserving endpoint alignment.   We benchmark PCF on controlled transports, sample-efficiency studies, and high-dimensional alignment tasks. PCF matches the accuracy of kernel MMD on the controlled benchmark and on raw-pixel MNIST--USPS at a cost linear in the batch size, and it is more sample-efficient, achieving lower endpoint error than MMD in 13 of 15 paired runs across library sizes. By avoiding pairwise kernel evaluation, it reduces discrepancy memory by 29.8% and trains at batch sizes where kernel MMD exceeds GPU memory. These results show that PCF provides accurate and efficient invertible transport, particularly in sample- and memory-limited settings.



### Backbone Matters: Benchmarking Encoder-Decoder Networks for Semantic Segmentation of Driving Scenes on BDD100K
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26191v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26191v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:39:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:39:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Ehsan Rassekh
- **Comment**: 6 pages, 3 figures, 4 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Semantic segmentation gives an autonomous vehicle a dense, pixel-level description of its surroundings and is a core part of scene understanding. Most segmentation networks consist of an encoder (the backbone) and a decoder, yet the backbone is often chosen without a systematic comparison. This work studies this choice on the BDD100K driving dataset. Four widely used decoders (U-Net, FPN, LinkNet and PSPNet) are combined with 13 backbones from six families (ResNet, VGG, DenseNet, Inception, MobileNet and EfficientNet), and all 52 models are trained with one common protocol. The best model, an FPN decoder with an EfficientNet-B4 backbone, reaches 62.6% mean intersection over union (mIoU), 89.8% accuracy and a loss of 0.092. Only two of the 52 models, both FPN with EfficientNet, exceed the 56.9% mIoU reported with the BDD100K benchmark. The effect of the backbone depends strongly on the decoder: the same EfficientNet-B4 gives 42.6% mIoU with PSPNet but 62.6% with FPN, and the gap between the best and the worst backbone ranges from 12.0 points (U-Net) to 42.5 points (FPN). The results and the scope of this study are discussed.



### A version of the Cloude/van Zyl decomposition for full/quad pol synthetic aperture radar data
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26195v1

- **Comment**: 11 pages, 5 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Based on the Cloude/van Zyl decomposition of reflection symmetric polarimetric SAR data we present a version for full/quad polarisation data. We demonstrate both versions on X-band F-SAR data over Vejers, Denmark. Differences between the original and our version of the decomposition occur especially for very bright man-made targets. These differences come with lower entropy for our version giving an improved focus on a more pronounced dominant scattering mechanism for these objects. Interpretation of the scattering mechanism related to the full/quad pol version of the decomposition is facilitated by means of the Cloude/Pottier $\barα$ angle.



### Learning to Think with Images Faithfully via Adaptive Data Selection
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26199v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26199v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:43:17+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:43:17+00:00
- **Authors**: Yu Zhao, Jiaxin Zhang, Rohit Saxena, Fazl Barez, Pasquale Minervini, Yiwen Guo
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Thinking with images (TwI) empowers vision-language models (VLMs) to overcome perceptual bottlenecks by actively cropping and zooming into fine-grained visual details, promising both higher accuracy and interpretable, region-grounded reasoning. However, our analysis shows that the improved accuracy of TwI models does not guarantee faithful tool use; they frequently invoke tools without actually relying on the zoomed region, undermining the reliability of tool-assisted reasoning. We trace this unfaithful behaviour to the training data: when questions are answerable without zooming, the model can answer correctly while ignoring the returned evidence, and this problem is exacerbated during training as the model becomes better at answering without tools. To keep zooming useful throughout training, we adapt the training data to the model's evolving capability and train on questions where correct zoomed evidence can still help the model answer correctly. This selection is supported by a data synthesis pipeline that localises small objects and constructs verifiable questions about their fine details, and complemented by an adaptiveness reward that favours tool-free answers when zooming is unnecessary. The experimental results show that our method substantially improves reasoning faithfulness and achieves state-of-the-art performance on visual search benchmarks (+11.6% on V*, +7.9% on HR-Bench 4K, and +9.4% on HR-Bench 8K). On multimodal hallucination benchmarks, our model also outperforms the compared TwI baselines. These results suggest that improving TwI faithfulness does not sacrifice accuracy and that both can be learnt from the right training data without directly supervising the reasoning process.



### Learn, Freeze, and Transfer an Activation Function Across Architectures
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26201v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26201v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:44:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:44:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Arun Prakash A
- **Comment**: 17 pages, 5 figures, 10 tables. Code: https://github.com/AI4Bharat/LeFT-activation-transfer, Interactive figures: https://arunprakash-a.github.io/papers/learn-freeze-transfer/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Transfer learning typically reuses knowledge acquired by a source network through its learned weights. We investigate whether a learned activation function can also be transferred and remain useful in networks trained from scratch. We study this complementary form of transfer through a learn-freeze-transfer protocol, in which the activation is optimized jointly with a source network in a single training run, and is then frozen and reused in target networks trained from scratch. A pointwise activation with globally shared parameters is independent of layer dimensions and can therefore be incorporated into networks of different architectures trained from scratch. We instantiate this protocol with FAct, a five-parameter activation represented by a two-harmonic truncated Fourier series, initialized from a Fourier approximation of GELU and learned in a Vision Transformer (ViT) on ImageNet-1K. When reused in a ViT of the same architecture trained from scratch on ImageNet-1K, the frozen activation (Frozen FAct) improves top-1 accuracy by 1.46 percentage points over GELU. Across four smaller target architectures (standard ViT, Swin Transformer, linear-attention ViT, and MLP-Mixer) and four image-classification datasets, it achieves the highest mean accuracy among eleven activation functions in 15 of 16 settings under a common training configuration, with improvements of up to 1.71 percentage points over the best conventional activation. When the learning rate and weight decay are tuned separately for each activation under the same search budget, Frozen FAct still ranks first in 11 of the 16 settings, second in four, and fourth in one. These results provide empirical evidence that a learned activation function can retain its usefulness when transferred across vision architectures, independently of the weights with which it was learned.



### SpecWM: Turning Verification into Refinement for Streaming World Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26211v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26211v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:50:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:50:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiaming Li, Lingzhen Li, Xiaze Yang, Chenyu Zhu, Kaiqi Liu, Xiaotong Zhao, Songlian Li, Toyota Li, Alan Zhao, Haobin Zhong, Yue Ma, Zhiyuan Ma
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recently, video world models have advanced interactive generation, with large models offering higher quality and smaller models enabling faster inference. Existing speculative methods combine their strengths through fast draft generation and selective target regeneration guided by rewards on decoded frames. However, verification covers only the external scorer's evaluation dimensions and adds decoding and scoring costs. Moreover, these scores provide no correction, requiring costly target regeneration for rejected drafts. To address these limitations, we propose Speculative World Model (SpecWM), a unified, training-free inference framework for streaming world models. It verifies draft chunks using the target's latent velocity field. Verification also yields a reusable correction; two thresholds select Accept, Refine, or Reject. Specifically, one-step refinement reuses it to improve perceptual quality, while three-step refinement strengthens camera following and trajectory consistency. These findings motivate transition-aware refinement. On WBench Navigation, SpecWM improves visual quality at 1.63$\times$ speedup over the target and raises NavScore from the draft's 75.99 to 85.63, achieving the best quality-speed-control trade-off. Additionally, our LongSpecBench shows sustained quality and control over long, multi-turn rollouts, while experiments on Hy-Nomas validate SpecWM across backbones and denoising budgets.



### MixReact: Physics-Based Reactive Humanoid Control from Multimodal Cues via a Discrete-Continuous Mixture of Transformers
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26218v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26218v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 14:57:08+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 14:57:08+00:00
- **Authors**: Bin Li, Xing Zhu, Jingya Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Humanoid agents in everyday environments are expected to react to diverse cues as humans do, with semantically appropriate and physically executable behaviors. Existing cue-driven reaction generation methods support only a narrow range of cue modalities and are limited to purely kinematic generation, leaving the resulting reactions susceptible to physical artifacts. To this end, we present MixReact, the first unified framework for physics-based reactive humanoid control from text, audio, and video cues, integrating a Discrete--Continuous Mixture of Transformers (DisCo-MoT) for state prediction with a History-aware Action Transformer (HAT) for physical execution. DisCo-MoT couples a discrete masked-modeling branch with a continuous autoregressive diffusion branch via one-way mixture attention within a MoT architecture. Guidance from discrete context mitigates collapse into averaged behaviors in one-to-many reaction generation, while direct prediction in continuous state space bypasses the decoding bottleneck of discrete representations. HAT then maps the predicted next state and observed execution history to executable actions, enabling robust closed-loop control in physics-based simulation. Experiments across all three modalities demonstrate consistent improvements over the baselines in fidelity, diversity, and physical consistency, while ablations validate the complementary contributions of the components. Our code will be released to facilitate future research. Project page: https://binlee26.github.io/MixReact_Page/



### Attention Drift Regularization with Adaptive Initialization for Domain-Robust Visual Recognition
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26234v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26234v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 15:09:20+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 15:09:20+00:00
- **Authors**: Shivangi Rai, Vinod K Kurmi
- **Comment**: Accepted in WACV 2027
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Source-free domain adaptation (SFDA) adapts a pretrained source model to unlabeled target domains without source data access, yet two critical failure modes remain unaddressed. First, attention drift: source-learned attention maps shift toward spurious or background features under distribution shift, destabilizing feature representations. Second, domain-gap-agnostic initialization: applying a single fixed source model uniformly across all target domains ignores domain gap severity, causing premature overfitting to noisy pseudo-labels under large shifts. We propose DGA-CAFC to address both failures. The Counterfactual Attention-based Feature Consistency (CAFC) module enforces feature stability by minimizing the discrepancy between representations computed under learned attention and those under counterfactual-inspired normalized perturbations, thereby promoting domain-robust features independent of spurious attention shifts. The Domain-Gap Aware Source Model Selection (DGASS) module estimates domain gap severity via target prediction entropy and selects the source model variant whose confidence level best matches the estimated gap, reducing negative transfer and stabilizing pseudo-label quality. Crucially, CAFC transfers beyond SFDA: applied as a lightweight regularizer to standard domain generalization objectives without target data. Extensive experiments across SFDA and DG benchmarks demonstrate that our method consistently improves over recent baselines, demonstrating the effectiveness and generality of the proposed framework. Project page is available at https://visdomlab.github.io/DGA_CAFC



### Beyond Covers and Titles: Evidence-First Ranking for Robust Cold-Start Recommendation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26249v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26249v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 15:25:37+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 15:25:37+00:00
- **Authors**: Fuyi Wang, Yuxin Cao, Yifan Liao, Renyang Liu, Lin Li, Wei Song
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Short-video platforms rank new uploads before anyone has watched them, using content towers that read a video's cover, title and frames. Existing attacks on content-aware recommenders perturb pixels or features and ignore platform re-encoding, and existing defenses train against norm-bounded perturbations that neither match real edits nor preserve recommendation quality. We show with REAP, a realizable attack through {a simulated platform channel (H.264 transcoding, rescaling, cropping and JPEG)}, that packaging edits raise a cold video by 32.0 rank-percentile points in unrelated audiences. We then observe that cold-start ranking does not need the packaging at all: a tower that sees only the video body ranks cold items best. Building on this, we propose Evidence-First Ranking (EFR), which scores cold videos from their body, admits packaging only through a support-gated residual bounded by $γ$, and merges cold and warm rankings by rank percentile, yielding an exact certificate against any packaging edit. On MicroLens-100K, EFR cuts the attack gain by 81% and raises cold-start Recall@100 from 0.1133 to 0.170, while keeping the merged NDCG@10 within 4% of the undefended system.



### Prism: Hierarchical Text Detection with Minimalist Design
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26254v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26254v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 15:27:43+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 15:27:43+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuchen Su, Xuesheng Wang, Zhilong Ji, Jinfeng Bai, Shijian Lu, Zhineng Chen
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Hierarchical text detection aims to localize words, text lines, and paragraphs while parsing their structural relationships. It provides essential structural cues for visual text understanding. To maximize accuracy, existing methods typically rely on complex multi-scale feature fusion necks and heavy-weight heads, resulting in bloated architectures and low inference efficiency. In this paper, we argue that a sufficiently pre-trained backbone can effectively replace these sophisticated external modules, while simple geometric inclusion suffices for hierarchical text structural parsing. Thus, we propose Prism, a minimalist framework for hierarchical text detection. Prism performs multi-granularity detection by attaching parallel lightweight heads to a single-scale backbone feature map, covering words, text lines, and paragraphs, and reconstructs the structural hierarchy via an efficient geometric inclusion algorithm. In addition, we systematically compare representative lightweight head archetypes and show that pixel-level regression offers the best accuracy-efficiency trade-off in this setting. Experiments on multiple public benchmarks show that our significantly simplified architecture achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance with remarkable efficiency. Specifically, it runs $1.7\times$ faster than LRANet on CTW1500 while achieving higher accuracy, and surpasses the current SOTA method, OmniParser V2, by over 6 PQ points on the hierarchical HierText dataset. Code will be released soon.



### Digitization of Analogue Gauges using Computer Vision
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26262v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26262v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 15:33:49+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 15:33:49+00:00
- **Authors**: Erico Kayumba Mutonkole, Olasupo Ajayi, Omowunmi Isafiade
- **Comment**: 9 pages, 12 figures. Presented at SATNAC 2025
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Analogue gauges remain prevalent in manufacturing industries for measuring physical quantities, yet manual monitoring by operators introduces human error and operational inefficiency. This research developed and evaluated an automated gauge reading system using computer vision and deep learning techniques. Three hybrid approaches were implemented and compared: OpenCV with Scikit-Image (traditional computer vision), OpenCV with ResNet18, and OpenCV with VGG19 (deep learning approaches). A comprehensive dataset of 1,848 pressure gauge images (0-10 BAR range) was curated through web scraping, synthetic generation, and augmentation techniques. The experimental results demonstrate that ResNet18 significantly outperforms the alternatives, achieving an accuracy of 97.3%, compared to VGG19's 64.4% and Scikit-Image's 63.8%. The study validates the effectiveness of residual learning architectures for industrial gauge digitization, providing a robust foundation for Industry 5.0 automation applications.



### Tracking Objects Through Assembly and Decomposition
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26269v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26269v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 15:38:13+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 15:38:13+00:00
- **Authors**: Cheryl Lam, Yihong Sun, Bharath Hariharan
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Tracking objects through transformations is essential for understanding real-world dynamics. Recent work has extended traditional object tracking to detect state changes. However, existing methods require an initial object prompt and detect only its decompositions, missing assembly events, which begin with multiple objects rather than one. We therefore introduce a prompt-free formulation of this task, where the goal is to recover all object tracks and detect both decomposition and assembly events given only a video. Our key insight is that assembly is decomposition in reverse. Building on this insight, we present Atlas, a zero-shot system that constructs and jointly reasons over forward and reverse spatiotemporal partitions to recover object tracks and their transformations. We further introduce OmniST, a benchmark with dense annotations of objects and both transformation types. On OmniST, Atlas recovers more than twice as many state changes as prior methods while remaining competitive on existing benchmarks that only evaluate decomposition.



### BADAS-3.0: Real-Time Driving Perception and Planning from One Camera
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26294v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.CL
