- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26294v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 15:49:40+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 15:49:40+00:00
- **Authors**: Roni Goldshmidt, Hamish Scott, Lorenzo Niccolini
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We ask how far a driving model can get with one camera and real-time latency. BADAS-3.0 is a 1B-parameter video encoder with four small heads. From two seconds of one forward camera, it predicts the current speed, the metric path just driven, a collision-risk score and six candidate futures over 6.4 seconds, in under 50 ms. Given the car's current speed, BADAS-3.0 has 44% lower minADE$_6$ over five trajectory benchmarks than the 34B Alpamayo 2 Super, which reads six cameras and runs over 100x slower. The gain comes from camera rigs that neither model was trained on. On the rig they share, the two are on par. Existing benchmarks are mostly nominal driving, so we add two open-loop safety benchmarks, one of real dashcam collisions and near-misses and one of Euro NCAP scenarios. On both, BADAS-3.0 has a higher pass rate than every published planner we tested.



### SKILL.PNG: Learning Agent Skills in Pixel Space
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26296v2
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.LG**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26296v2)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 15:51:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-20 09:01:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Maximilian Seeth, Chengzhi Martin Hu, Marie Kempkes, Paul Röttger, Leo Schwinn
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: AI agents are commonly specialized for new tasks and domains using skills, i.e. natural language instructions placed in their context. However, the discrete nature of textual skills prevents continuous optimization. Gradient-based approaches such as finetuning, on the other hand, are typically model-specific, preventing skill transfer across different agents. Since many agents today are built on vision-language models (VLMs), we can address these limitations by introducing Skill$.$png: a method for obtaining skill-like image inputs whose pixels are optimized with gradient descent through the vision encoder of a frozen VLM. Image skills are compatible with any VLM, and a single image can be optimized jointly for an ensemble of models. We show that Skill$.$png significantly outperforms state-of-the-art textual skills optimized using frontier LLMs in 9 of 16 settings (across 4 VLMs and 4 benchmarks) while requiring only 64 to 256 image tokens instead of roughly 900 to 2000 text tokens at inference time. Hybrid skills, i.e. text and image skills jointly optimized, further improve agent performance. Moreover, an image optimized with an ensemble of VLMs can successfully transfer across the models in the ensemble. Skill$.$png thus presents the first agent skill that can be optimized with gradient descent while retaining transfer benefits of text skills. Our code is available at: https://github.com/omseeth/skill.png



### C-LiTE: A Lightweight Foundation Model for Cellular feature learning in Histopathology
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26316v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26316v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:03:54+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:03:54+00:00
- **Authors**: Esha Sadia Nasir, Jiaqi Lv, Gozde N. Gunesli, Shan E Ahmed Raza
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Characterising individual cells is fundamental to computational pathology, as cellular morphology informs tasks such as tumour grading, mitotic assessment and immune profiling. However, most pathology foundation models are trained on tissue patches and capture cellular features only implicitly, while existing cell-level models are either restricted to specific cell populations or rely on computationally expensive architectures with hundreds of millions of parameters. Applying ViTs directly to small cellular crops also presents an architectural challenge: upsampling increases computational cost without adding morphological information and may introduce interpolation artefacts. To address these limitations, we introduce C-LiTE, a lightweight foundation model for cellular representation learning in H&E-stained histopathology, with only 22 million parameters. C-LiTE employs a native-resolution tokenisation strategy tailored to cellular inputs, processing 64 x 64 px cell-centred crops using a 4 x 4 patch embedding to produce a 16 x 16 token grid without image upsampling. To transfer visual knowledge from large-scale pre-training, we adapt the patch embedding weights of a pre-trained ViT-5-S using a pseudoinverse-based transformation, enabling weight initialisation at the finer patch resolution. We further assemble a pre-training dataset of 60 million cell-centred crops from TCGA, balanced across 19 cancer types and 16 tissue types. To our knowledge, this represents the largest public dataset for self-supervised pre-training on H&E images at the cellular level. We evaluate C-LiTE on seven public datasets spanning mitotic figure recognition, nucleus and cell-type classification, and immune cell phenotyping. Under frozen-feature evaluation, C-LiTE achieves a mean macro-F1 of 0.670, approaching CytoFormer (0.682) with approximately 30x fewer parameters.



### ResVision: Spectrum-Matched Resizing and Context-Preserving Inspection for Visual Reasoning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26318v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26318v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:04:30+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:04:30+00:00
- **Authors**: Guojian Zhan, Jiahao Xu, Yahui Liu, Yao Hu, Chuan Mu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Agentic visual reasoning improves vision-language models by selectively enlarging evidence without adding new scene content, but the benefit depends on whether fine structure remains accessible and whether local observations stay grounded in the full scene. We identify three recurring bottlenecks: high-frequency detail can be attenuated during visual-token formation; spatially distributed evidence can remain encoded yet difficult for the language model to retrieve; and isolated crops can weaken whole-detail correspondence through scale and positional mismatch. ResVision addresses these limitations with two complementary components. Spectrum-Matched Resizing (SMR) estimates image complexity and selects an adaptive global sampling scale, avoiding unnecessary enlargement on simple inputs while allocating more visual tokens to detail-rich images. Context-Preserving Inspection (CPI) returns each inspected crop together with the complete working view, preserving local detail and global context across tool turns. We evaluate Direct and Agentic variants on four frontier VLMs and 29 benchmarks spanning six visual-reasoning families. Complete ResVision (Agentic Base plus SMR plus CPI) is best for every model, improving by 3.36 to 4.94 points over Direct Base and 1.87 to 2.94 points over Agentic Base. These results show that adaptive visual sampling and context-preserving inspection address distinct, complementary constraints in multimodal reasoning.



### FirstCross: Convex Single-Photon 3D Reconstruction from Sparse Views
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26321v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, eess.IV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26321v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:05:47+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:05:47+00:00
- **Authors**: Chi-Wei Lee, Cheng-Han Chiang, Yi-Chun Chang, An-Kai Chen, Po-Chih Kuo
- **Comment**: Project page: https://arthur031221.github.io/firstcross/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Single-photon lidar records the arrival time of each returning photon, enabling 3D reconstruction from few views and scarce photons. However, leading neural methods such as Transientangelo require over an hour of non-convex per-scene optimization, and their accuracy degrades as photon flux decreases. We propose FirstCross, a network-free approach that instead solves a short sequence of convex problems on a voxel grid. For a solid object, the Poisson likelihood of a ray with given signal and background rates depends on the scene solely through its first crossing, the point where the ray first enters the object. Decomposing this likelihood yields two tractable terms. The first penalizes surfaces in front of detected photons and admits a tight convex closure, namely volume rendering with a running-maximum transmittance in place of NeRF's product transmittance. The second accounts for photons that no surface explains and admits a convex upper bound once the object is assumed to be thick behind each detected surface point. Compared with Transientangelo under its own real-data protocol, FirstCross achieves lower depth error across every test scene, view count, and flux level. With three views and 100 times fewer photons than at the protocol's lowest flux, our mean object depth error stays under half of Transientangelo's with all photons. Given our detected surface points, the best classical fusion has 1.3 to 1.8 times our three-view error across flux levels. On real room-scale captures with 1% of their photons, TransientNeRF has 2.8 to 7.7 times our error. FirstCross lets few-view single-photon scanners cut exposure to 1% and reconstruct an object in about a minute on one GPU.



### Online Temporal Rotations for 2 Bit KV-Cache Quantization in Video Diffusion Transformers
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26334v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26334v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:14:13+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:14:13+00:00
- **Authors**: Ryan Lucas, Petros Prastakos, Rahul Mazumder
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video diffusion transformers reuse key--value (KV) caches across denoising steps, which quickly becomes a bottleneck for long video generation. We find that quantizing temporally distant cache vectors leads to a large range and high quantization error, but that nearby video tokens in the KV-cache share a persistent pattern per channel. These patterns also vary across videos, motivating rotations that are adapted to each video's unique cache. We propose Temporal Rotation Quantization (TRQ), a method that rotates frames in the cache that are adjacent in time before quantization. For each block of temporally adjacent cache vectors, TRQ fits an orthogonal transform that maps the block's per-channel means to a vector that is close to a constant across channels. To keep the quantization format lightweight, we parameterize this rotation as a tree of $2 \times 2$ planar rotations, each stored with only two bits, followed by a fixed Hadamard transform shared across all blocks. The rotations are fit without gradients or changes to model weights, and we prove that a simple greedy fit is globally optimal for our 'mean-flattening' objective. The transforms are fit online for each new video, and we release fused Triton kernels to make this process efficient at inference. At 2-bit precision and matched compression, TRQ improves VBench subject consistency over the state-of-the-art method QVG from 90.38 to 92.02 on LongCat-Video-13B and from 86.33 to 87.61 on HY-WorldPlay-8B, as well as improving other metrics.



### OffBeat: Multicoset Sampling for 4D Capture Beyond the Camera Frame Rate
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26347v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, eess.IV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26347v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:20:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:20:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Chi-Wei Lee, An-Kai Chen, Cheng-Han Chiang, Yi-Chun Chang, Po-Chih Kuo
- **Comment**: Project page: https://arthur031221.github.io/offbeat/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: For 4D reconstruction, a synchronized multi-camera rig records all views at the same timestamps and misses the motion between them. Staggered triggers spread the same frames over more timestamps. However, when the cameras along each row fire one after another, as in 4DSloMo's schedule on the N3DV rig, the row acts as one moving camera that confounds depth with motion. We introduce OffBeat, which models a staggered rig as a spatially distributed multicoset sampler: shifted copies of one sampling grid. From the calibration alone, OffBeat derives camera groups that fire together and their firing order. Since one group records each timestamp, a short-lived primitive can fit an error its cameras share, such as a color offset, and flicker in novel views. We show that a primitive's opacity over time weights all groups equally, for every number of groups, only if it lasts at least one camera frame interval. On Spacetime Gaussians, a minimum lifetime approximates this bound, and a closed-form smoothed box of that length satisfies it exactly, with less flicker than a Gaussian in its place at nearly equal PSNR. In simulated staggered capture on N3DV, OffBeat outperforms the full 4DSloMo pipeline with video diffusion on every test frame, by 3.04 dB in mean dynamic-region PSNR. Between the first and last synchronized frame, it outperforms the best synchronized method of each scene, with or without frame interpolation, by 1.98 dB in mean dynamic-region PSNR, while synchronized capture is better at its own frames. On PanopticSports, in this frame range, OffBeat is comparable in PSNR to synchronized capture with interpolation. On every N3DV scene, replacing 4DSloMo's schedule with ours raises the dynamic-region PSNR of Spacetime Gaussians, 4DSloMo without diffusion, and our reconstruction. Multi-camera rigs could follow faster motion at the same frame count by changing only when each camera fires.



### Contactless Estimation of Respiratory Frequency Using 3D-CNN on Thermal Images
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26367v1
- **DOI**: 10.1109/JBHI.2025.3567141
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26367v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:33:11+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:33:11+00:00
- **Authors**: Federica Gioia, Filippo Pura, Alberto Greco, Dario Piga, Arcangelo Merla, Marco Forgione
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: IEEE Journal of Biomedical and Health Informatics, 29(10), 7387-7395, 2025
- **Summary**: Monitoring physiological parameters such as respiratory rate ($f_R$) is essential for diagnosing and managing various pathological conditions. Thermal imaging offers a promising contactless alternative to traditional methods, which often rely on partially invasive sensors or obtrusive wearable systems. However, existing approaches for $f_R$ estimation from thermal signals typically require extensive pre-processing and manual or semi-automatic region-of-interest (ROI) tracking, limiting their practical applicability. This study proposes a deep learning-based method for estimating $f_R$ directly from thermal videos, eliminating the need for complex pre-processing and ROI tracking. A 3D Convolutional Neural Network (3D-CNN) is developed to operate on raw thermal video data. To address challenges related to small datasets, the model is trained using data augmentation and transfer learning from synthetic datasets. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed approach achieves a validation $R^2$ score of approximately 0.61 on both pre-processed and raw thermal videos. By simplifying the workflow, this method holds promise for enhancing the feasibility of thermal imaging in real-world applications, such as remote healthcare and driver monitoring in automotive applications.



### Fast Self-Supervised Generative CT Denoising via Few-Step Sampling
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26370v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26370v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:34:29+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:34:29+00:00
- **Authors**: Xingyi Yao, Zhengtao Ma, Zhennong Chen
- **Comment**: 30 pages, 4 figures, 2 tables. Xingyi Yao and Zhengtao Ma contributed equally to this work. Zhennong Chen is the corresponding author
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Low-dose and thin-slice computed tomography (CT) images suffer from elevated noise that obscures anatomical detail, while clean references for supervised denoising are often unavailable. Although Noise2Noise permits self-supervised training from noisy pairs, direct regression tends to oversmooth fine structure. Averaging multiple independently generated samples mitigates this blur, though repeating multi-step diffusion sampling is costly. We introduce Noise2Noise with Improved Mean Flow (N2N-iMF) to accelerate this process via few-step sampling. N2N-iMF learns to generate one noisy image conditioned on its paired counterpart; at inference, independent outputs are sampled and averaged to suppress noise. We evaluated image quality and sampling efficiency on simulated thin-slice brain and low-dose abdominal CT across four sampling methods and averaging counts from 1 to 20, and validated the approach on real-world photon-counting CT (PCCT) brain scans. With ten samples per conditioning input and three network evaluations per sample, N2N-iMF achieved a mean absolute error of 2.60 HU on brain CT and 14.02 HU on abdominal CT, outperforming 50-evaluation denoising diffusion implicit model (DDIM) sampling (2.91 and 14.53 HU). On the same GPU, per-output generation time dropped from 7.29 to 0.43 s. Across eight PCCT cases, N2N-iMF reached a mean gray-white matter contrast-to-noise ratio of 1.341, versus 0.916 for Noise2Noise and 1.176 for DDIM. N2N-iMF accelerates self-supervised generative CT denoising without distillation while keeping the number of averaged outputs adjustable at inference.



### EgoCaptioner: Towards Comprehensive and Fine-Grained Egocentric Video Captioning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26380v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26380v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:39:59+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:39:59+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuqi Tang, Yang Shi, Yuran Wang, Tengfei Liu, Rundong Wang, Zongyang Qiu, Qixun Wang, Zhuoran Zhang, Xuanyu Zhu, Yifan Xu, Yiyan Ji, Shujie Li, Shixuan Liu, Haotian Wang, Yuanxing Zhang, Pengfei Wan, Chengwei Qin
- **Comment**: 17 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Egocentric video captioning requires fine-grained detail and accurate timing, yet current multimodal large language models (MLLMs) often miss brief actions, confuse their order, or misinterpret the wearer's motion. Existing caption formats also offer limited support for combining detailed narration with precise timing. We present EgoCaptioner, which pairs a video-level overview of the setting, participants, and the wearer's overall activity with timed steps whose narration adapts to varying perceptual demands and marks key moments at specific timestamps. We develop a coarse-to-fine annotation pipeline that establishes event structure before expanding step descriptions, yielding 50,000 training examples from eight egocentric video sources. Complementary rewards assess factual accuracy and coverage, measure step-level temporal alignment, and penalize fragmented or redundant narration, guiding caption refinement through Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO). To improve the reliability of factual supervision, we supplement caption references with pose-derived motion facts when sensor data are available. Across five egocentric benchmarks, EgoCaptioner achieves leading performance among evaluated open-source models in caption quality, caption-conditioned question answering, and temporal alignment. It also surpasses Gemini 3.1 Pro on PhysCapBench and all evaluated proprietary baselines on Minerva-Ego under the caption-to-QA protocol. Ablations support the complementary roles of factual and temporal rewards and show that coarse-to-fine annotation provides more effective supervision than single-pass annotation.



### SEEC: A Large-Scale Dataset and Benchmark for Self-Supervised External-Eye Representation Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26386v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26386v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:41:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:41:48+00:00
- **Authors**: George R. Nahass, Michelle Fleishaker, Sanjay Ganesh, Sasha Hubschman, R. V. Paul Chan, Chad A. Purnell, Pete Setabutr, Ann Q. Tran, Sathya N. Ravi
- **Comment**: 36 Pages, 10 Figures, 9 Tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: External-eye photography captures clinically relevant periocular anatomy at low acquisition cost, yet no large-scale standardized resource exists for evaluating self-supervised external-eye representations. We introduce a 4.35-million-image Standardized External-Eye Corpus (SEEC) derived from fourteen heterogeneous public face datasets, together with a benchmark for external-eye representation learning. For evaluation, we provide held-out evaluation distributions, landmark-annotated anatomical probe sets, and clinical disease-classification experiments spanning ptosis, thyroid eye disease, and craniofacial syndromes. We evaluate representations at two complementary levels: (a) the representation level, by assessing embedding geometry across multiple data distributions, and (b) the functional level, by assessing anatomical recovery, out-of-distribution anatomical transfer, and disease classification. Our models include ResNet-101 and ViT-B encoders pretrained using InfoNCE, VICReg, and LeJEPA objectives and compared with DINOv2 and MAE. Our results indicate that representation-level and functional-level evaluations are dissociable: broader embedding-space utilization does not consistently correspond to anatomical transfer or disease-classification performance, and no single encoder is universally optimal. Domain-specific external-eye encoders outperform DINOv2 and MAE on cross-dataset anatomical transfer despite being pretrained at substantially smaller scale, while external-eye-pretrained ViT models match or outperform these general-purpose baselines on frozen disease-classification probes. We release benchmark code, evaluation scripts, and preprocessing pipelines to reproduce SEEC at https://monkeygobah.github.io/seec-project/



### GateTrace: Where Do Crowd Crossings Go Missing Under Occlusion?
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26388v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26388v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:42:45+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:42:45+00:00
- **Authors**: Ao He, Qianchen Yu, Vivian W. H. Wong
- **Comment**: 23 pages, including supplementary material; 3 figures
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: At large events, pedestrian tracking supports entrance and passageway counts, but totals hide which crossings were missed. In a stadium study, the system recorded about four in five clearly visible crossings but fewer than one in three mostly obscured crossings. The videos lacked trajectories annotated across frames, so we turned to MOT20, which provides pedestrian trajectories and visibility. We introduce GateTrace to locate missed crossings and assess changes to counting rules. It derives reference crossings from annotations under fixed rules, matches reports by person, direction, time and position, and traces misses through saved boxes and counting decisions. With a shared detector, ByteTrack, OC-SORT and BoT-SORT recover 38.9-46.7% of low-visibility reference crossings on development sequences, versus 98.1-99.0% at high visibility. The gap recurs on two further sequences under a protocol fixed in advance. Published outputs of MOT20-tuned BASE recover 63-78% at low visibility across both sequence groups. Losses stop at different stages across systems. Across four systems and both groups, 47-79% of low-visibility misses have no nearby output linked to the person or no possible crossing in the saved tracks. Removing later checks from BASE on development sequences leaves most misses unresolved and lowers precision against the fixed reference list from about 96% to 77%. Misses also remain among sampled reference crossings that two human annotators supported after checking the videos. GateTrace shows where crossings are lost and measures the recovery and precision costs of changing later checks. We will release the code and per-crossing records.



### ViDecide: Teaching Video Diffusion Models to Reason and Plan Step by Step
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26389v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26389v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:43:42+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:43:42+00:00
- **Authors**: Kaiwen Zhang, Yiming Gao, Zeqi Xiao, Xintao Wang, Pengfei Wan, Kun Gai, Xingang Pan
- **Comment**: Project page: https://kevin-thu.github.io/ViDecide
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Current leading video generation models can synthesize highly realistic videos, while still frequently violating physical and logical constraints in multi-step reasoning and planning, even after training with large-scale reasoning data or existing diffusion RL methods. We attribute these failures to the conventional video training paradigm, which treats a solution as a fixed-length clip rather than a chain of decisions. We present ViDecide, a decision-centric framework that trains video diffusion models to predict state transitions causally and decide when to stop, with a decision-focused training schedule. We further propose Corrective Fine-tuning (CFT), which corrects failed decisions by mapping their noise to the model's own successful continuations, achieving stable improvement without policy-gradient optimization. Across abstract games, planning tasks, and a new benchmark of multi-step robot planning, ViDecide achieves an average strict success rate of 83.8%, far above 23.3% for standard fine-tuning and 67.8% for the strongest prior method, and generalizes to larger puzzles and unseen layouts. Our framework therefore unlocks greater potential for video generation models as reliable reasoners and planners in the visual world. Project page: https://kevin-thu.github.io/ViDecide.



### TacInno: Teaching Vision-Language-Action Models the Contact Dynamics Beyond Vision and Action
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26405v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26405v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:53:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:53:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Weile Li, Yunfan Lou, Muqing Shi, Zihao Zhang, Zhenbiao Huang, Boxian Yao, Xiyu Zhu, Zhibo Pang
- **Comment**: 11 pages, 4 figures, 4 tables. Project page: https://tacinno.github.io
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have recently been equipped with tactile sensing to perceive visually subtle contact events in contact-rich manipulation. However, adding tactile input yields inconsistent gains, and some visuo-tactile VLA models rely little on tactile input, because much of the tactile change, such as the gel deformation during gripper closure, is predictable from visual observations and executed actions. To address this problem, we introduce TacInno, which isolates the contact dynamics that vision and executed actions cannot explain, termed tactile innovation, and uses tactile innovation only as a training target. Specifically, the TacInno tokenizer, conditioned on visual observations and executed actions, compresses the future tactile change into a discrete code, and the policy, TacInno-VLA, learns to predict the code, adding no module for the code at deployment. TacInno-VLA achieves average success rates of 87.5% across six simulation tasks and 70.5% across four real-world tasks, outperforming the strongest visuo-tactile baseline by 10.7 and 9.0 percentage points, respectively. Ablation studies validate the contributions of visual and action conditioning, multi-frame encoding, and auxiliary code prediction. Together, these results demonstrate the effectiveness of tactile innovation as a supervision signal for contact-rich manipulation.



### Training Vision Models for LLM-Driven Weight-Space Programming
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26408v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26408v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:56:27+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:56:27+00:00
- **Authors**: Timo Ropinski, Sebastian Kranz
- **Comment**: Project page: https://viscom.uni-ulm.de/wsp
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: It has been demonstrated that, in certain settings, large language models can perform weight-space programming to directly modify the weights of neural networks. However, it is unknown how dedicated training of these networks can contribute to the successes of weight-space programming. We present a benchmark of LLM-driven weight-space programming on vision models, in which five LLMs edit convolutional networks and vision transformers on relabeling and error-correction tasks on Waterbirds, CIFAR-100, PACS and Entity-30. We report more than 2,600 such edit processes, whereby each edit is scored on held-out images for success, side effects and accuracy loss. We compare standard training with label smoothing, gradient-based editable training, and our Edit-Ready Training (ERT), a novel method that rehearses the direct, closed-form weight edits LLMs perform. We show that preparing networks for editing raises the success of LLM edits substantially where standard networks are hard to edit. ERT further achieves the highest mean score for GPT-6 Astra and Claude Opus 5.5 across all evaluated baselines. We will release our LLM testing framework alongside all tasks, editing trajectories and scores. Project page: https://viscom.uni-ulm.de/wsp



### WeMM-Video: Pushing the Frontiers of Open-Source Omni-Modal Video Understanding
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26409v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, eess.AS
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26409v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 16:58:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 16:58:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Changli Tang, Jie Yang, Tianyi Wang, Lei Li, Daoze Zhang, Zijie Xin, Zhan Liu, Li Li, Jinchang Xu, Fengyun Rao, Chao Zhang, Jing Lyu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Video understanding, particularly with accompanying audio, remains a central challenge in multimodal intelligence. In this report, we introduce \textbf{WeMM-Video}, a family of Chinese-English bilingual omni-modal foundation models spanning 2B, 4B, 9B, and 35B-A3B variants. Centering on joint audio-visual perception and reasoning, WeMM-Video unifies text, image, audio, and video processing within a hybrid-thinking paradigm, seamlessly supporting both a non-thinking instruct mode for fast, direct responses and a thinking mode for explicit step-by-step reasoning. The models are trained on a large-scale multimodal corpus of approximately 1.2 trillion tokens through progressive pre-training and a multi-stage post-training pipeline incorporating multi-teacher OPD, anti-repetition MrDPO, and specialist RL. Evaluated across more than 120 benchmarks, WeMM-Video demonstrates superior performance in omni-modal video understanding and reasoning, while maintaining highly competitive capabilities across general audio, silent video, image, and text tasks. Specifically, in omni-modal video evaluations, our flagship WeMM-Video-35B-A3B establishes a new open-source state of the art, substantially outperforming proprietary models such as Qwen3.5-Omni-Flash and Seed2.0-Mini-0428 while narrowing the gap with the strong Gemini-3.0-Flash, and our compact WeMM-Video-9B surpasses the larger open-source Qwen3-Omni-30B-A3B-Instruct. We publicly release the WeMM-Video family along with our evaluation and reproduction pipeline to foster further research and development in the open-source community.



### Awaken-HI: Scaling Humanoid Intelligence with Whole-Body Human Experience
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26420v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26420v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:03:03+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:03:03+00:00
- **Authors**:  EngineAI
- **Comment**: project page: https://engineai-robotics.github.io/Awaken-HI
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: A central goal of humanoid robotics is for robots to perform the everyday work people do in homes and workplaces. Existing egocentric video datasets cover many of these activities but provide little synchronized whole-body motion data for learning loco-manipulation at scale. We build Awaken-Human, which pairs approximately 50,000 hours of egocentric stereo video with synchronized whole-body human motion and covers more than 100 skills. Building on this, we also introduce Awaken-HI (Humanoid Intelligence), a vision-language-action model for humanoid loco-manipulation that jointly predicts original human motion and retargeted robot actions through a shared action expert. We show that loco-manipulation skills transfer more effectively from people to humanoids when human trajectories directly supervise the shared action expert. Increasing human pretraining data reduces held-out robot action prediction error according to an empirical scaling law and raises the mean success rate on downstream real-robot tasks. After full-corpus pretraining and T800 adaptation, Awaken-HI achieves a mean success rate of 93.75% across eight individual tasks and outperforms GR00T N1.7 and $π_{0.5}$ in both individual and long-horizon evaluations. The same policy generalizes to objects unseen during robot post-training and to new pairings of familiar objects and destinations.



### MemFolder: Structure-Guided Memory Folding for Efficient Agent Memory Management
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26427v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CL**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26427v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:05:55+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:05:55+00:00
- **Authors**: Xuyang Liu, Chenwen Lu, Junxi Wang, Siyu Ren, Xinying Lin, Siteng Huang, Hongli Sun, Linfeng Zhang, Honggang Chen
- **Comment**: Code is available at https://github.com/xuyang-liu16/MemFolder
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Long-term agents accumulate large external memory stores as interactions unfold, increasing the costs of storage, retrieval, and response generation. Efficiency is constrained by two coupled factors: a growing record count increases retrieval overhead, while longer retrieved contexts increase prefill computation and decoding-time attention costs. Existing methods prune or consolidate records, but pruning can discard useful evidence, whereas consolidation may produce fewer yet longer retrieval units. We introduce MemFolder, a structure-guided memory folding framework that sequentially reduces record count and context length. The framework comprises two core components that share memory groups, using cross-record structure to guide both evidence consolidation and compact representation. RecordFolder grounds entity mentions, progressively selects representative yet complementary anchors, and combines semantic and entity affinity with temporal chaining to consolidate compatible records into fewer retrieval units. ContextFolder encodes the original records within these groups into compact text and uses verification feedback to revise encodings when key facts cannot be recovered. Experiments on the three text-based memory benchmarks LoCoMo, PersonaMem, and LongMemEval and the two multimodal benchmarks M3-Bench-web and M3-Bench-robot demonstrate the strong performance of our methods. Notably, on Mem0, RecordFolder retains 99.0% of the original average performance across the three text-based benchmarks with 50% fewer records, exceeding the strongest baseline by 1.9 percentage points in performance retention. Combined with ContextFolder, existing record compression methods further shorten their output records while retaining most of their performance.



### FFGaussType: Feed-Forward Conversion of Glyph and SVG Geometry into Compact Gaussian Assets
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26431v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.GR
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26431v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:08:21+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:08:21+00:00
- **Authors**: Haoan Feng, Tianfu Wang, Peimeng Guan, Dae Yeol Lee, Guan-ming Su
- **Comment**: 20 pages. Project page: https://fengyee.github.io/FFGaussType/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Immersive 3D content allows text and graphical elements, such as subtitles, annotations, and icons, to be placed inside a scene rather than displayed as flat overlays. Placed this way, they respect occlusion and perspective and take on user-assigned materials and lighting while preserving their original shapes. In Gaussian scenes, however, each new glyph or icon requires its own iterative optimization, which limits scalable content creation. We present FFGaussType, which converts extruded glyph and SVG outlines into Gaussian assets in a single forward pass. A point encoder maps anchors sampled on the known surface to a single feature plane, from which a decoder shared by all assets predicts neural Gaussians carrying surface normals. This separates geometric conversion from material and lighting choices, and the feature plane supports image and video coding for compact delivery. We construct a paired dataset of 27,221 glyph and SVG icon assets with anchors and calibrated normal and silhouette renderings, holding out font families for evaluation. Conversion from prepared anchors takes 9.1 ms per asset, at a fraction of the compute and memory of prior baselines. On unseen font families and test icons, FFGaussType achieves $1.62^\circ$ normal error and $0.985$ silhouette IoU, the best among the compared feed-forward converters, matching the fidelity that per-asset fitting needs thousands of steps to reach. The code and dataset will be released after publication.



### AdaPixFlow: Learning Continuous-Time 3D Dynamics Across Scenes from Sparse Multi-view Observations
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26433v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26433v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:09:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:09:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Hrishikesh Viswanath, Aniket Bera
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Understanding how objects move in dynamic scenes requires recovering their 3D motion from visual observations and predicting their positions at unobserved times. Existing methods either optimize scene-specific velocity fields or impose predefined physical and kinematic assumptions. We instead study learning a continuous-time dynamics operator across scenes, mapping multi-view visual observations to 3D object kinematics. We introduce Adaptive Pixel Flows (\ours{}), which learns this operator from multi-view RGB-D observations without per-scene optimization or predefined motion models. The method fuses observations from all cameras into a shared voxel grid in world coordinates, from which the network predicts a continuous-time velocity field. Because the operator is trained across scenes, it learns motion priors that carry over to unseen scenes. The field can then be integrated from observed states to predict object positions between observations and beyond the observed sequence. On held-out PhysInOne scenes, \ours{} improves F1 over the strongest competing method by 2.5\% for in-between prediction and 15\% for future prediction. Compared with per-scene velocity-field methods, the improvements are 30\% and 85\%, respectively. Applied zero-shot to Spring-Gaus, it improves future-prediction F1 by 12\%.



### HumanCOIN: A 12,500-Hour Dataset for Human Intent in Egocentric Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26434v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.RO
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26434v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:09:25+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:09:25+00:00
- **Authors**: Shihan Wu, You Liu, Chunyu He, Minghui Qin, Longhan Jiao, Jinbang Guo, Shaoxuan Xie, Kaixi Tian, Wenjie Chen, Jingrui Pang, Yuanhang Zhang, Yichi Zhang, Miao Liu, Tengfei Pan, Yance Jiao, Yunyan Zhang, Yihang Li, Yongjian Shen, Li Liu, Kai Zhu, Chen Fang, Jianxing Chen, Sihan Liu, Xiaofeng Wang, Jie Zhao, Jing Li, Yueqi Wang, Yichen Tang, Wei Li, Zhexuan Yang, Yue Cao, Ji Zhao, Kai Wang, Dongsheng Jiang, Yongwei Wang, Wei Mu, Jiangmiao Pang, Maoqing Yao, Wenchao Ding, Zheng Zhu, Zhongpu Xia, Dalong Du, Jianwei Niu, Dahui Gao, Zhiwei Yu, Bo Lei, Huan Yang, Yonghua Lin, Tiejun Huang, Zhongyuan Wang, Guocai Yao
- **Comment**: 41 pages, 10 figures, Project page: https://flagopen.github.io/HumanCOIN
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: First-person manipulation datasets treat the observation-action pair as the atomic unit and compress the generation and execution of an action into a policy network's weights, rendering the intent state behind the action unobservable. Human motor control research shows that intent mediates between observation and action and leaves observable traces along the intent pathway from decision to effect: gaze reaches the target before the hand, muscle load builds before contact, and cortical activity precedes the motor command. To record this pathway at scale, we present HumanCOIN, a first-person manipulation dataset built around human intent: 12,500 hours across 38 scene classes with three tiers of proxies. At the output end, the L1 attention tier (10,000 h) infers gaze from headband camera video, exposing the operator's next object of attention and reach before the hand moves. Midway, the L2 exertion tier (2,000 h) records surface EMG from armbands, making load expectations readable before execution is complete. At the origin, the L3 cognition tier (500 h) records scalp EEG, giving early readouts of task decisions and monitoring. Each tier inherits all proxies from the tiers below. The dataset further provides multi-view RGB images, depth maps and point clouds, 6-DoF head and wrist pose, and step-level action and object annotations. The controlled L3 setting additionally measures gaze, hand pose and finger kinematics directly. Clock and calibration data are released with the dataset so that any read rate can be re-aligned. The three tiers cover three stations, from visual orienting to cortical preparation, making the process behind visual-action pairs available for modeling at dataset scale. Project page: https://flagopen.github.io/HumanCOIN.



### Asuka-FM: A System-Architectural Co-Design Framework for Efficient Text-to-Image Pretraining on Consumer Hardware
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26437v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26437v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:12:34+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:12:34+00:00
- **Authors**: Víctor Rodrigo González, Pablo M. Olmos, Ignacio Peis, Alejandro Lancho
- **Comment**: 40 pages, 19 figures, 17 tables. Project page: https://deeplearningcafe.github.io/projects/asuka-fm ; Code: https://github.com/deeplearningcafe/asuka-fm
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Frontier text-to-image (T2I) foundation models predominantly rely on multi-billion parameter Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) pretrained across massive compute clusters, creating an accessibility barrier for academic laboratories. When training DiTs from scratch on single consumer GPUs (e.g., an NVIDIA RTX 4090 with 24\,GB VRAM), practitioners encounter three primary bottlenecks: quadratic attention sequence costs, Just-In-Time (JIT) compilation graph breaks induced by variable aspect-ratio bucketing, and an autoencoder throughput wall where frozen Variational Autoencoder (VAE) operations dominate step latency. In this work, we introduce \textbf{Asuka-FM}, an end-to-end system and architectural framework demonstrating that competitive dual-stream DiTs ($\sim$206M parameters) can be pretrained from scratch on a single consumer GPU in $\sim$160 GPU hours for under \$55. Asuka-FM integrates four core designs: (i) an AdaLN-free dual-stream DiT backbone reducing parameter count; (ii) structured sequence sparsification via Sparse-Dense Residual Fusion (SPRINT) to cut attention FLOPs by over 50\%; (iii) continuous aspect-normalized Rotary Position Embeddings (RoPE) coupled with shifted square crop training, guaranteeing static tensor shapes while unlocking zero-shot virtual camera control; and (iv) a pixel adaptation phase utilizing a lightweight (10M parameter) Patch Detailer Head operating under velocity regression ($v$-prediction). Evaluated on open-domain benchmarks without benchmark-specific fine-tuning, Asuka-FM achieves GenEval 0.588 / DPG 78.96 scores at 256px and GenEval 0.609 / DPG 78.51 scores at 512px, setting a new Pareto-optimal efficiency standard for democratic foundation modeling.



### Risk-Controlled Test-Time Adaptation for Vision-Language-Action Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26438v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26438v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:12:48+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:12:48+00:00
- **Authors**: Zixuan Liu, Hashem Haghbayan, Zizhan Zheng, Siavash Khajavi
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Retrieval-augmented vision-language-action (VLA) models enable efficient test-time adaptation by leveraging expert demonstrations as additional context without updating model parameters. However, even when retrieved demonstrations are relevant to the current task, the VLA may fail to effectively utilize the retrieved information, ultimately leading to task failure. To address this challenge, we propose R2-VLA, a risk-controlled retrieval-augmented VLA framework that enables reliable test-time adaptation by satisfying a user-specified task success rate requirement with minimal expert assistance. Specifically, R2-VLA learns an uncertainty predictor that estimates the uncertainty of the VLA's predicted actions given the retrieved demonstrations, guiding when to act autonomously or request expert assistance. We further introduce a task-level risk-control procedure that calibrates the uncertainty threshold to satisfy a user-specified task-failure risk requirement. Moreover, R2-VLA continually incorporates expert corrections into its retrieval memory to improve future adaptation while preserving the prescribed task-level risk guarantee. Experiments across three VLA backbones and four LIBERO benchmark suites demonstrate that R2-VLA improves average task success by 9.2--11.2 percentage points, achieving the desired target with fewer expert requests. These results highlight the effectiveness of connecting the usefulness of retrieved demonstrations with task-level risk control to enable successful robotic manipulation.



### Mind2Act World: A Diagnostic Benchmark for Embodied Mind, Physical Action, and Mind-Action Composition in Robotic Manipulation
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26444v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.AI, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26444v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:15:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:15:38+00:00
- **Authors**: Yuzhi Huang, Jie Wu, Fanding Huang, Junchao Lin, Weichen Zhang, Ziyi Xiong, Yang Li, Zhengwei Xie, Zhimin Wang, Zhen Liu, Jiazhen Huang, Yuzheng Zhang, Yuche Liu, Hongyi Yang, Chen Tang, Shixiang Tang, Hakan Aktas, Adam Wu, Duo Wu, Kangye Ji, Shuzhao Xie, Huanan Liu, Weijue Bu, Chenxin Li, Xinyuan Wang, Tianyi Bai, Yinan Mao, Jiawei Wang, Minglei Li, Heng Yu, Ehsan Adeli, Minchen Li, Yong-Lu Li, Cengiz Öztireli, Huazhe Xu, Chao Yu, Jing Zhang, Shanghang Zhang, Weitao Zhou, Wenwu Zhu, Cheng Chi, Jingyan Jiang, Zhi Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Embodied intelligence aims to let robots perform complex physical tasks with human-like adaptability and reliability, which requires three complementary capabilities: history-based cognitive reasoning, precise and timely physical execution, and the composition of reasoning and execution through environmental feedback. Yet existing benchmarks either evaluate reasoning and execution within the same tasks or assess them separately without their composition, so they reveal little about whether a failure comes from reasoning, execution, or their composition. We introduce Mind2Act World, a diagnostic benchmark of 30 simulated manipulation tasks from five real-world application domains. Each task has three difficulty levels and two scene settings (Clean and Random), yielding 180 configurations. The tasks form three views: Mind tests decisions based on past observations, Act tests precise and timely execution, and Mind2Act combines both in closed-loop tasks where action outcomes guide later decisions. Comparing the three views shows whether a system lacks a capability or fails to compose capabilities it already has. A diagnosis harness further attributes the failed episodes of three representative systems to Mind, Act, or their composition and summarizes each system in a profile card. Our evaluation of multimodal agents, vision-language-action models (VLAs), and world-action models (WAMs) shows that competence in a single view does not carry over to Mind2Act tasks. Even the strongest agent succeeds in 52.7% of Mind and 39.0% of Act episodes but in only 12.0% of Mind2Act episodes. The strongest agent retains task state but struggles with sustained contact, whereas end-to-end policies execute practiced motions but retain little task state. These findings point to composing reasoning and execution through environmental feedback as the main open problem.



### Fixing the Last Mile: Failure-Aware Image Restoration with Region-Specific Guidance
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26456v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26456v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:21:15+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:21:15+00:00
- **Authors**: Hongyu An, Fanghua Yu, Kainan Yan, Luc Van Gool, Chao Dong, Jinjin Gu
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Recent advances in image restoration have greatly improved global image fidelity, producing visually plausible results across most regions of an image. Nevertheless, even high-quality restorations often exhibit localized failures, including artifacts in semantically important regions such as faces and text, structural inconsistencies, and texture mismatches. Although these errors typically affect only a small portion of the image, they often dominate the overall perceptual quality of the result. In this work, we propose Failure-Aware Image Restoration (FAIR) to address the "last-mile" problem in image restoration. Rather than applying one uniform generative prior over the whole image, we condition a single-pass restoration model on both where local failures are likely to occur and what corrective guidance each region requires. This region-bound conditioning enables fine-grained and controllable restoration that improves semantic accuracy and stylistic consistency in critical regions while maintaining faithful, high-quality restoration elsewhere. Our method complements existing restoration pipelines by shifting the emphasis from global fidelity to local perceptual acceptability. Extensive experiments show that the proposed approach resolves common failure cases and improves perceptual quality, bridging the gap between technical accuracy and practical usability.



### SHUSH: Emission-Aware Reward Design for Proactive Video Assistants
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26478v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26478v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:38:18+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:38:18+00:00
- **Authors**: Shawn Liang, Soamar Homsi, Gourav Datta
- **Comment**: under review at ICASSP
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Proactive video assistants must decide not only what to say, but also when to speak. Existing reinforcement-learning objectives can reward timely, correct responses without accounting for response volume, thereby encouraging unnecessary or repeated emissions. We identify this failure mode in MMDuet2, whose proactive area-under-the-curve (PAUC) reward promotes timely responses but does not price the number of emissions. We show analytically that this makes additional responses weakly penalized or effectively free, systematically encouraging over-emission. We then propose an emission-aware F1 reward for interval-valued annotations, using exclusive span--emission matching to price false emissions and missed spans. Under MMDuet2's training recipe, our policy on the visual subset of OmniPro replies 2.5x less often than MMDuet2 while raising timing F1 from 0.259 to 0.396 and content-aware F1 from 0.103 to 0.154; a more conservative variant cuts replies by 82.5% and raises precision from 0.161 to 0.418. On ProactiveVideoQA, the same reward adds replies where the baseline is nearly silent, and PAUC ranks a heavily over-emitting policy second despite near-last F1, because it is insensitive to response count. Our results indicate that proactive assistants should be trained and evaluated with objectives that price emissions.



### SGAR: Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction via Sparse Geometry-Guided Attention
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26493v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26493v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:46:57+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:46:57+00:00
- **Authors**: Aoxiang Fan, Maria Pilligua, Corentin Dumery, Oliver Heinimann, Pascal Fua
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Feed-forward models have been very successful in 3D reconstruction with ad hoc attention designs that relate all view patches densely. However, the cost of this dense global attention grows quadratically with the number of views, preventing the model from applying to larger-scale reconstruction settings. In this paper, we argue that most dense attention computations are unnecessary from a classical structure-from-motion perspective: 3D structures stem from geometrically matching regions in 2D. We bring this principle to learning-based feed-forward reconstruction with our Sparse Geometric Attention Reconstruction (SGAR) method. In SGAR, each patch token attends to only a small set of patch tokens from other views, chosen by a lightweight learned key selection module trained to predict cross-view 3D proximity of tokens. Thus, dense global attention is replaced by much more efficient geometric-similarity-guided sparse attention. SGAR is a general method that can be applied to different feed-forward models by fine-tuning. In our experiments, we show that SGAR is the fastest among both state-of-the-art dense and sparse methods and the most accurate sparse one. It runs up to 12-times faster than dense attention at 1,000 views with stable pose accuracy where existing sparse methods fail, and shows the best performance in reconstructing more complete geometries on the same time budget.



### Visual Attribution Limits and Graded Provenance Auditing in Commercial Video-Generation APIs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26508v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CR**, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26508v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:56:06+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:56:06+00:00
- **Authors**: Haocheng Mei, Yuchuan Tian, Yingte Shu, Chao Xu, Kai Han, Hanting Chen, Yunhe Wang
- **Comment**: 23 pages, 2 figures, 13 tables
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Commercial video-generation APIs bill by model and tier, while intermediaries could substitute cheaper models, lower tiers, or cached clips. What can a delivered file establish about its origin? We combine measurements of passive attribution and cryptographic provenance into a graded audit. We test attribution probes and zero-shot detectors under re-encoding, downscaling, and frame-rate changes, and survey C2PA and GB 45438 metadata across 19 commercial endpoints. Only authenticated provenance supports accusations: tier contradictions or duplicate signed instances under fresh-generation contracts, excluding retries and idempotent returns. Unverifiable signed-endpoint deliveries trigger original-file demands under pass-through clauses; archive-reuse and delivery-parameter screens support review only. Six-model container and bitstream accuracy falls from 83.9% on raw files to 45.3% after uniform re-encoding, using different feature sets. Subsampling real references from 24 to 3 fps raises pooled D3 AUROC from 0.845 to 0.988. Only BytePlus authenticates tier claims under our pinned policy, including 187/187 audited deliveries across three Seedance tiers; tested remuxing and re-encoding leave processed Seedance files unverifiable. The audit identifies authenticated contradictions in 100/100 raw 720p signed tier-swap cases, with zero observed false flags on the tested honest pools.



### Direct3D-V3: Latent-Free Diffusion Transformers in the Native Voxel Domain
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26511v1
- **DOI**: 10.1145/3842524
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26511v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:58:20+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:58:20+00:00
- **Authors**: Shuang Wu, Jiachen Qian, Youtian Lin, Feihu Zhang, Philip Torr, Yao Yao, Xun Cao
- **Comment**: The Project Page is: https://dreamtechai.github.io/Direct3D-V3. The Paper is published in ACM TOG (SIGGRAPH Asia 2026)
- **Journal**: ACM Trans. Graph. 45, 6, Article 244 (December 2026), 18 pages
- **Summary**: Native 3D generation has largely been built around latent diffusion, with recent advances concentrating on stronger variational autoencoders (VAE) and increasingly elaborate latent representations, from vecset and triplanes to structured sparse voxels. Despite their architectural differences, these methods all share the same premise: geometry is first compressed into a latent space and only then modeled by the generator. We take a different route and propose Direct3D-V3, a latent-free diffusion transformer (DiT) framework that discards the VAE altogether and operates in the native voxel domain, directly modeling raw SDF voxel grids during both training and sampling. To make this direct voxel formulation geometrically reliable, we introduce an implicit manifold regularization over neighboring voxels to suppress non-watertight surfaces, and convert predicted SDF grids into meshes via Marching Cubes so that rendered normal losses can supervise the model directly from its predicted SDF. To improve alignment between the conditioning image and generated geometry, Direct3D-V3 also predicts the camera parameters of the conditioning image as an auxiliary task, encouraging the DiT to learn the mapping from image observations to 3D shape. This latent-free design removes the reconstruction bottleneck, brings training under an objective in the native voxel domain, and yields meshes with substantially richer local detail. The predicted camera and native SDF voxel output further make Direct3D-V3 extensible to generative reconstruction, where diffusion is interleaved with normal-guided refinement to improve alignment with the observed view while the generative prior completes unseen regions. Project page: https://dreamtechai.github.io/Direct3D-V3.



### Plan4D: Generative Plannable 4D Worlds
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26516v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26516v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 17:59:02+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 17:59:02+00:00
- **Authors**: Hangwei Zhang, Rujing Dang, Ruimin Lin, Jiahong Wu, Xiangxiang Chu, Chuanxia Zheng
- **Comment**: NeurIPS 2026, Project Page: https://fogradio.github.io/plan4d-project
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Most world models for future prediction prioritize aestetic quality of generated videos, but offer only weak physical grounding and limited geometric consistency over time, due to the lack of explicit 4D modeling. In this paper, we introduce plan4d, a possible next milestone towards building a generative plannable 4D world model that predicts future RGB videos together with dense 4D geometry, including depth, cameras, point clouds, and scene flow from an observed scene context and text-specified actions. Since training data with paired actions and dense 4D annotations is inherently scarce, plan4d leverages the complementary priors of foundational video generators and 3D reconstructors. Specifically, we propose a model-stitching architecture that couples Depth Anything 3 with LongCat-Video and augments it with an auxiliary DPT head for scene-flow prediction. The resulting model allows us to plan and generate complex, long-horizon, and fine-grained actions in a dynamic 3D scenes, while substantially improving both visual fidelity and geometric accuracy of the generated future videos. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world datasets demonstrate that plan4d achieves state-of-the-art performance in generative 4D modeling of environmental reactions. Project Page: https://fogradio.github.io/plan4d-project



### Deterministic Temporal Embedding for Calcium Imaging Trace Extraction
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26522v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG, eess.IV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26522v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 18:00:35+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:00:35+00:00
- **Authors**: Qi Gao, Julius Beichert, Miriam Ratliff, Karl Rohr
- **Comment**: 5 pages. Submitted to IEEE ISBI 2027
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Reliable extraction of calcium-dependent fluorescence traces in calcium imaging sequences requires identifying pixels whose fluorescence intensity reflects the same underlying cellular activity. We present a deterministic temporal embedding framework that maps pixel-wise fluorescence traces to a low-dimensional representation in which temporally coherent sources form spatially coherent regions. Starting from a fuzzy-neighborhood graph and spectral initialization, we optimize a class-balanced cross-entropy objective using exact sparse attraction and a deterministic particle-mesh decomposition of the dense repulsive interaction with sparse edge compensation, avoiding stochastic negative sampling. The resulting latent representation is spatially decoded into reliable source supports for trace extraction without imposing an explicit cellular shape prior. Experiments on synthetic and in vivo two-photon calcium imaging data demonstrate robust recovery of irregular and spatially touching sources together with efficient deterministic embedding optimization.



### AURA: AUdio-dRiven streaming Avatar
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26540v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26540v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 18:17:38+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:17:38+00:00
- **Authors**: Nathaniel Cohen, Nicolas Dufour, Amélie Royer, Alasdair Newson, Patrick Pérez
- **Comment**: BMVC 2026. Project page: https://kyutai.org/aura
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Current audio-driven video diffusion models can synthesize realistic talking-head videos but generally require dozens of diffusion denoising steps over multi-second video chunks, making them unsuitable for streaming applications such as voice assistants and virtual avatars, where low-latency and online causal generation are required properties. AURA generates video chunk by chunk with a rolling KV cache, requiring only the audio of the current chunk to start generating, rather than a multi-second audio look-ahead like bidirectional models. It comes in two sizes: AURA 1.3B streams 576p video in real time on a single GPU (20.7 fps for a 16 Hz playback rate), making it suited to interactive avatar applications, and AURA 5B streams 720p video at 9.7 fps. Our two-stage distillation pipeline first aligns the causal student with the teacher, by training it to match the teacher's trajectories with fewer diffusion steps and a chunk-wise sliding window causal attention mask, then applies Distribution Matching Distillation (DMD) with backward simulation to close the remaining quality gap. On standard talking-head benchmarks, AURA 1.3B runs 23x faster than its own bidirectional teacher and reaches a quality comparable to TalkVerse at a lower resolution, and AURA 5B matches its TalkVerse teacher at 720p while running 17x faster. To support reproducibility and further research on streaming avatar generation, we release the weights of both models along with a dataset of 30K synthetic videos and their precomputed teacher trajectories.



### Multi-institutional grading of Barrett's esophagus dysplasia without stain normalization using a pathology foundation model and multiple instance learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26562v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26562v1)

- **Authors**: Amirali Khosravi, Jaidip M. Jagtap, D. Chamil Codipilly, Catherine E. Hagen, Jason T. Lewis, Rickey E. Carter, Shahriar Faghani, Vani J. A. Konda, Fouad Otaki, Shajan Peter, Nicholas Shaheen, Sachin Wani, Prasad G. Iyer, Bradley J. Erickson
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Barrett's esophagus dysplasia grading suffers substantial inter-observer variability, particularly for low-grade dysplasia, complicating clinical decision-making. We conducted a retrospective multi-institutional diagnostic accuracy study of a foundation model-based deep learning system for dysplasia grading, using 969 H\&E-stained whole slide images from four institutions. Patch-level classifiers trained on expert annotations identified diagnostically relevant regions, followed by attribute-aware multiple instance learning for slide-level classification as non-dysplastic Barrett's esophagus (NDBE), low-grade dysplasia, or high-grade dysplasia. On a held-out test set (n = 130), macro-averaged AUC was 0.96 (95\% CI 0.93--0.98). For binary dysplasia detection, sensitivity was 98\% (80/82) and specificity 81\% (39/48), with a negative likelihood ratio of 0.030 and quadratic-weighted \kap{} of 0.83 against expert consensus. No high-grade dysplasia case was misclassified as NDBE. Performance was consistent across institutions without stain normalization, indicating that stain-robust foundation-model representations may remove the site-specific preprocessing that has constrained prior systems to institution-specific deployment.



### Text-Aware, Vision-Blind: How Multimodal Image Editing Systems Handle Unsafe Inputs
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26569v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CR**, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26569v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 18:36:19+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:36:19+00:00
- **Authors**: Ziqing Yang, Yixin Wu, Michael Backes, Yang Zhang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Multimodal image generation systems have made rapid progress and increasingly emphasize content safety. However, their safety mechanisms tend to rely on a single modality and lack joint consideration of both visual content and text prompts, particularly in instruction-guided image editing, where the uploaded image may itself carry risk. We thus introduce EditSafetyBench, a benchmark that measures system-level safety awareness of advanced image editing systems, containing 5,940 image-instruction pairs, covering safe and unsafe images across 11 risk categories and three edit intents. We then probe the benchmark dataset on four systems, including Gemini, GPT, Qwen API, and Qwen Local. Based on our evaluation, we observe a systematic text-vision safety mismatch: Gemini and GPT refuse 87.1\% and 81.6\% cases containing unsafe-intent editing prompts, while only refusing 24.2\% and 22.4\% cases with both unsafe images and neutral instructions. At the category level, most risks are considered inconsistently by different systems, e.g., GPT allows 70.4\% editing attempts of unsafe political content while Gemini only allows 34.8\%. Notably, sexual content shows the strongest visual sensitivity, likely benefiting from mature NSFW identification works. Our findings highlight the need for safety guardrails that jointly reason over both visual and textual modalities in image editing systems.



### Probing the "Conscious" Mind: Selective Access and Global Workspace in Vision-Language Models
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26590v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.AI**, cs.CV, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26590v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 18:46:50+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:46:50+00:00
- **Authors**: Jiahao Huang, Jun Wang
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Vision-language models (VLMs) integrate visual and textual information, but their outputs do not reveal internal concept representations. Global workspace theory (GWT) proposes that information is selected from parallel brain processes, integrated in a shared workspace, and made available to specialised processes. We introduce the Multimodal Concept Lens (MC-Lens), a multimodal Jacobian lens, to study these functions in VLM concept processing. Its vision encoder and language decoder lenses map intermediate states to a shared semantic space defined by the decoder's output vocabulary to compare concept accessibility across modalities. MC-Lens supports internal concept interventions to examine the role of accessed concepts in downstream computation. (i) Accessibility and selective access. Multimodal concept information is readable in intermediate states and selectively accessible as tasks require. We extend "readout carriers" from one name token to a token set to better capture and express concept information and support counterfactual experiments. (ii) Concepts in downstream computation. Counterfactual experiments show that accessed concept information participates in downstream computation and reasoning. (iii) Development of cross-modal alignment and selective access. We develop two J-space metrics tracking the development of cross-modal concept alignment more clearly than hidden state cosine in controlled VLM training. The experiment also shows that reliable selective access develops later than concept accessibility. (iv) Shared functions across models. Across VLM families and sizes, we find shared patterns of concept accessibility, query-dependent selectivity and cross-task intervention effects despite differences in internal organisation. These findings offer an experimental basis to study GWT in multimodal artificial systems and how it can guide cross-modal alignment improvements.



### Coda-DaVinci: Reimagining Video Generation with Code2Video
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26595v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26595v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 18:51:58+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:51:58+00:00
- **Authors**: Yiyan Ji, Qixun Wang, Yang Shi, Zhuoran Zhang, Yifan Xu, Tengfei Liu, Yuqi Tang, Xiaohan Zhang, Shujie Li, Jiafu Tang, Xuanyu Zhu, Xinyu Liu, Yan He, Yushuo Guan, Zihao Wang, Xianghua Ying, Shixuan Liu, Haotian Wang, Wenjing Yang, Zhaoxiang Zhang, Yuanxing Zhang, Pengfei Wan, Jiaheng Liu
- **Comment**: 47 pages
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Code2Video uses agent-authored 3D proxies to guide video generation, enabling explicit control over structure and dynamics. However, how to construct proxies and prompts for optimal downstream quality remains underexplored, as do the capabilities and limitations of this paradigm. Through controlled experiments and blinded human evaluation, we study proxy granularity, physics simulation, and prompting. The results reveal task-dependent trade-offs: coarse proxies favor natural motion, while medium-grained proxies favor consistency. Physics simulation and proxy-aligned prompting further improve average quality. Guided by these findings, we introduce Coda-DaVinci, a task-adaptive agentic framework for Code2Video generation that selects between proxy-guided and direct generation, adapts modeling granularity to each entity, and coordinates simulation with proxy-grounded prompting. Its multi-agent harness preserves scene assets and guides repairs through visual inspection and independent review. Experiments demonstrate leading instruction following on VTR and scene consistency on R2M, improved physical plausibility over direct generation, and higher visual realism than the compared Code2Video baselines. Long-video evaluations further show gains in event structure and consistency across successive interactions, while revealing limitations in transition stability and adherence to scene settings.



### Depth Pro 2: Sharp and Temporally Consistent Metric Monocular Depth
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26603v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.LG
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26603v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 18:55:41+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:55:41+00:00
- **Authors**: Peiyun Hu, Marko Mihajlovic, Lyndon Duong, Lahav Lipson, Wei Dong, Amaël Delaunoy, Stephan R. Richter, Vladlen Koltun
- **Comment**: Code and weights available at https://apple-aiml-research.github.io/ml-depth-pro-2/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: We present Depth Pro 2, an approach to estimating dense metric depth for images and video at full resolution. By analyzing prior methods for monocular depth estimation, we find that they commonly trade off temporal consistency and boundary sharpness: multi-frame methods favor consistency, single-frame methods favor sharpness. To overcome this trade-off, we train a single, scalable network that processes both images and video and predicts depth maps at native resolution. Depth Pro 2 produces depth maps that are metrically accurate, sharp, and temporally consistent all at once, outperforming prior work in all of these dimensions. We release a family of models that define a new Pareto frontier in terms of metric accuracy, sharpness, temporal consistency, and runtime. We release code and weights at https://apple-aiml-research.github.io/ml-depth-pro-2/



### AgentRoboCritic: Understanding the Capability of Frontier Agents on Direct Robot Control
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26613v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.RO**, cs.CL, cs.CV
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26613v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 18:57:25+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:57:25+00:00
- **Authors**: Yiyang Du, Yuxuan Kuang, Qitao Zhao, Yixuan Wang, Zhanqiu Guo, Yi Xu, Xin Li, Wenbin He, Liu Ren, Chenyan Xiong
- **Comment**: 28 pages. Project page: https://adu2021.github.io/blog/AgentRoboCritic/
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: Frontier multimodal language agents such as GPT-6 Astra have demonstrated strong capabilities in controlling robots directly via low-level numerical actions without high-level primitives. However, existing benchmarks report only episode-level success rates, which show neither how these agents succeed nor where they fail. In this paper, we present a systematic action-level analysis of frontier agents on direct robot control across 12 manipulation benchmarks. We introduce AgentRoboCritic, an intent-grounded critique framework in which the policy agent states a local intent alongside each action, and a critic evaluates the action's outcome and failure mode against this intent. Our action-level analysis demonstrates the promising capabilities of frontier agents on direct robot control, including closed-loop recovery from local execution failures, reliable semantic grounding across diverse scenes, and strong zero-shot manipulation without task-specific adaptation. It also identifies their current limitations, including task-relevant physical execution bottlenecks such as grasping and alignment, and uneven recovery across failure modes. Finally, we validate our critic on a 400-action human-annotated benchmark and release a dataset of 12,844 annotated actions alongside a fine-tuned open-source critic, Muse-Glimmer-30B, whose failure-mode accuracy rises from 32.58% to 74.24%, enabling low-cost action-level analysis.



### ScaleWorld: High-Resolution World Generation through Shared Prediction and Refinement
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26615v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**
- **Links**: [PDF](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26615v1)
- **Published**: 2026-11-19 18:58:04+00:00
- **Updated**: 2026-11-19 18:58:04+00:00
- **Authors**: Yansong Qu, Wesker Zheng, Quanjian Song, Zhiyuan Zhang, Xiao Cao, Shengchuan Zhang, Liujuan Cao
- **Comment**: None
- **Journal**: None
- **Summary**: High-resolution generation enables video world models to render fine-grained scene structures and textures, but directly generating high-resolution video incurs substantial computational and memory costs. We present ScaleWorld, a causal video model that unifies low-resolution world prediction and high-resolution refinement within a single shared backbone. By refining its own predictions, ScaleWorld produces high-resolution world videos without requiring a separate refinement model. For each video chunk, the shared backbone first completes a low-resolution prediction and then performs one-step high-resolution refinement conditioned on that prediction. To support efficient streaming generation, we maintain separate causal states with a long low-resolution history for prediction and a short high-resolution history for refinement. To address task interference during distillation, we separate specialist optimization from capability consolidation and introduce task-asymmetric on-policy distillation (TA-OPD). This procedure transfers prediction and refinement behaviors from specialized teachers into a shared student through local velocity matching under their respective sampling and history policies. {Extensive experiments demonstrate that ScaleWorld achieves high-quality world generation with improved computational and memory efficiency.



### One Prior, Two Dynamics: Repurposing Video Diffusion Transformers for Self-Supervised Latent Action Learning
- **Arxiv ID**: http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26617v1
- **DOI**: None
- **Categories**: **cs.CV**, cs.AI, cs.LG, cs.RO
